Independent of the spin-flip argument. Direct corollary `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_odd_card_eq_zero`: combinatorial proof of Z₂ symmetry from the closed form alone (no `correlation_odd_vanish`). Λ-layer + ℤ^d wrappers `high_temp_numerator_filter_eq_empty_of_odd_card_{Λ,latticeGraph}`, `correlationΛ_{,latticeGraph_}high_temp_h_zero_odd_card_eq_zero`. Along-exhaustion + ℤ^d wrappers `correlationAlongExhaustion_{,latticeGraph_}high_temp_h_zero_odd_card_eq_zero`, `high_temp_numerator_filter_eq_empty_of_odd_card_alongExhaustion{,_latticeGraph}`. **Correlation nonnegativity from FV (3.46)**: `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_nonneg` — alternate derivation of GKS-I at `h = 0` from the closed form (numerator/denominator both ≥ 0 when `tanh(βJ) ≥ 0`). Λ-layer + ℤ^d wrappers `correlationΛ_high_temp_h_zero_nonneg`, `correlationΛ_latticeGraph_high_temp_h_zero_nonneg`. Along-exhaustion + ℤ^d wrappers `correlationAlongExhaustion_{,latticeGraph_}high_temp_h_zero_nonneg`. **A = ∅ consistency check** `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_empty_A`: at `A = ∅` the FV (3.46) closed form correctly reduces to `1`, matching `correlation_empty`. Requires `0 ≤ β·J` for denominator positivity. Λ-layer + ℤ^d wrappers `correlationΛ_{,latticeGraph_}high_temp_h_zero_at_empty_A`. Along-exhaustion + ℤ^d wrappers `correlationAlongExhaustion_{,latticeGraph_}high_temp_h_zero_at_empty_A`. **Correlation closed form** `correlation_high_temp_expansion_h_zero_closed`: `⟨σ_A⟩_{β,0} = (∑_{X : ∂X=A} tanh^{\|X\|}) / (∑_{X : ∂X=∅} tanh^{\|X\|})` (FV §3.7.3 eq. (3.46)). Λ-layer wrapper in `AmbientLattice/Defs.lean`; the ℤ^d Λ-layer and along-exhaustion wrappers now live in `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/HighTemperatureBounds.lean`. Along-exhaustion ambient wrapper `correlationAlongExhaustion_high_temp_expansion_h_zero_closed` now lives in `AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/HighTemperatureBounds.lean`. **General external-field correlation expansion** `correlation_high_temp_expansion_general_h_subset_form` (`Conditioning/CorrelationClosed/GeneralField.lean`): the general-`h` counterpart `⟨σ_A⟩_p = (∑_{X ⊆ E} tanh(βJ)^{\|X\|} ∑_σ σ_A (∏_{e ∈ X} σ_e) exp(βh ∑ σ_i)) / (∑_{X ⊆ E} tanh(βJ)^{\|X\|} ∑_σ (∏_{e ∈ X} σ_e) exp(βh ∑ σ_i))`. Unlike the `h = 0` case there is no parity collapse — the field factor `exp(βh ∑ σ_i)` blocks the `{±1}` cancellation; the shared `cosh(βJ)^{\|E\|}` prefactor cancels (needs only `cosh(βJ) ≠ 0`, no denominator-nonvanishing hypothesis, via `inv_mul_cancel₀`). Λ-layer wrapper `correlationΛ_high_temp_expansion_general_h_subset_form` (`AmbientLattice/Defs/HighTempPartition/ClosedForms.lean`) and along-exhaustion wrapper `correlationAlongExhaustion_high_temp_expansion_general_h_subset_form` (`AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/HighTemperatureBoundsExpansionClosedCorrelation.lean`) lift it through `correlationΛ = correlation (inducedGraph G Λ)`. (GJ §18.3/§18.5.) **Master arbitrary-observable form** `gibbsExpectation_high_temp_expansion_general_h_subset_form` (`Conditioning/CorrelationClosed/GeneralField.lean`): for any observable `F : Config ι → ℝ`, `⟨F⟩_p = (∑_{X ⊆ E} tanh(βJ)^{\|X\|} ∑_σ F(σ) (∏_{e ∈ X} σ_e) exp(βh ∑ σ_i)) / (∑_{X ⊆ E} tanh(βJ)^{\|X\|} ∑_σ (∏_{e ∈ X} σ_e) exp(βh ∑ σ_i))` — the canonical GJ §18.3 high-temperature representation of thermal averages, of which the correlation form above (`F = spinProduct A`) and the magnetization (`F = spinProduct {i}`) are immediate specialisations. Public numerator helper `numerator_observable_high_temp_expansion_subset_form` (the `F = 1` case is `partitionFunction_high_temp_expansion_subset_form`); the correlation form is now a one-line corollary. Along-exhaustion lift `gibbsExpectationAlongExhaustion_high_temp_expansion_general_h_subset_form` (`AmbientLattice/GibbsExpectationHighTempExpansion.lean`) for the infinite-volume limit. (GJ §18.3/§18.5.) **External-field response in subset form** `hasDerivAt_correlation_field_high_temp_subset_form` and `hasDerivAt_magnetization_field_high_temp_subset_form` (`PhaseTransition/FieldResponseHighTemp.lean`): the GJ §17.6 fluctuation-response derivative `∂⟨σ_A⟩/∂h = β(⟨σ_A M⟩ − ⟨σ_A⟩⟨M⟩)` (`M = ∑_i σ_i`) with each thermal average expanded through the master subset form — connecting the §5.3/§17.6 susceptibility/response machinery with the §18.3 high-temperature expansion; the magnetization version (`A = {i}`) gives the local field susceptibility `∂⟨σ_i⟩/∂h`. (GJ §17.6, p. 313; §18.3, pp. 330–331.) **Pair+singleton bundle** `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_singleton_bundle`: under `0 ≤ β·J`, packages `⟨σ_i⟩ = 0`, `0 ≤ ⟨σ_iσ_j⟩`, `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩ ≤ 1` into a single triple. Full 5-layer wrap with Λ / along-ex / ℤ^d / ℤ^d-along-ex companions. **Ferromagnetic bundle** `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_singleton_bundle_ferromagnetic`: under `0 ≤ J, 0 < β`, the same triple from `mul_nonneg hβ.le hJ`. Full 5-layer wrap with Λ / along-ex / ℤ^d / ℤ^d-along-ex companions in the same PR. **Trivial-slices full bundle** `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_singleton_trivial_slices_bundle`: packages the four `J=0` and `β=0` consistency vanishings (singleton at J=0, singleton at β=0, pair at J=0, pair at β=0) into one statement. Full 5-layer wrap. **Complete-summary bundle** `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_singleton_complete_summary`: under `0 ≤ β·J`, single statement bundling pair upper bound, pair sandwich lower, singleton vanishing, and pair vanishing at `J=0` / `β=0` trivial slices. Useful as a single import for downstream applications. Full 5-layer wrap. **Z complete-summary bundle** `partitionFunction_high_temp_expansion_h_zero_complete_summary`: under `0 ≤ β·J`, single statement bundling Z lower bound, upper bound, and trivial-slice values `Z⟨0,0,β⟩ = Z⟨J,0,0⟩ = 2^|ι|`. Λ / along-ex / ℤ^d Λ / ℤ^d along-ex companions in same PR. **freeEnergy complete-summary bundle** `freeEnergy_high_temp_h_zero_complete_summary`: under `0 < |ι|` and `0 ≤ β·J`, single statement bundling free-energy lower bound, upper bound, and trivial-slice values `f⟨0,0,β⟩ = f⟨J,0,0⟩ = log 2`. Λ / along-ex / ℤ^d Λ / ℤ^d along-ex companions in same PR. **Single-edge tanh lower bound** `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_ge_tanh_div_two_pow_edges`: under `0 ≤ β·J` and an edge `s(i, j) ∈ G.edgeSet`, `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^{⟨J,0,β⟩} ≥ tanh(β·J) / 2^|E|`. The single edge `e = s(i,j)` contributes `tanh(β·J)` to the FV (3.46) numerator (key lemma `singleton_edge_mem_high_temp_pair_filter`); denominator ≤ `2^|E|`. First quantitative non-trivial lower bound on the pair correlation between adjacent sites. Direct from the lower bound + `Real.tanh_pos`. Strengthens GKS-I in this specific setting. | §18.4 analogy | Cluster expansion machinery (lattice version) | **Conditional / limited-range: foundations + main bijection + Z identity + general-`t` Mayer–Montroll identity — Issue #1499 — complete: `mayer_identity_general_t` holds in the convergence regime, with the unconditional eventual form near `t = 0`; see the Mayer–Montroll Phase C entry at the end of this cell** | `IsEvenSubgraph`, `IsPolymer`, `polymerSupport`, `IsPolymerVertexDisjoint`, `IsCompatiblePolymerFamilyVertexDisjoint`, `edgeComponent`, `polymerDecomposition` (`ClusterExpansion.lean`); the bijection `evenSubgraphs G ↔ vdCompatiblePolymerFamilies G` is established in both directions. Capstone `partitionFunction_high_temp_expansion_h_zero_polymer_family`: `Z(J,0,β) = 2^|ι|·cosh(β·J)^|E|·∑_{Γ ∈ vdCompatiblePolymerFamilies G} ∏_{P ∈ Γ} tanh(β·J)^|P|`. **Mayer expansion foundation** (Step 576): `PolymersIncompatible P Q := ¬ Disjoint (polymerSupport P) (polymerSupport Q)` with `Decidable` instance, `symm`, `iff_not_isPolymerVertexDisjoint`, `iff_exists_shared_vertex`, and `self_of_isPolymer` (a non-empty polymer is incompatible with itself, reflecting the multi-set convention for clusters). **Incompatibility graph** (Step 577): `incompatibilityGraph : SimpleGraph (Finset (Sym2 ι))` defined via `SimpleGraph.fromRel PolymersIncompatible`, with `incompatibilityGraph_adj` characterising adjacency as `P ≠ Q ∧ PolymersIncompatible P Q` (the disjunction in `fromRel` collapses by symmetry of `PolymersIncompatible`) and `DecidableRel` instance. **Cluster polymer set** (Step 578): `IsClusterPolymerSet G Γ := Γ.Nonempty ∧ (∀ P ∈ Γ, IsPolymer G P) ∧ ((incompatibilityGraph).induce ↑Γ).Connected` — the set-level notion of cluster (no multiplicity yet). `IsClusterPolymerSet.singleton`: any singleton `{P}` of a polymer is a cluster set (vacuous Preconnected on a single vertex). **Polymer-sequence incompatibility graph** (Step 579): `polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph (ω : α → Finset (Sym2 ι)) : SimpleGraph α` parameterised by an arbitrary index type `α`, with adjacency `i ~ j ↔ i ≠ j ∧ PolymersIncompatible (ω i) (ω j)` and `DecidableRel` instance. The identity-indexed sequence on the polymer space recovers Step 577 (`polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph_id`). **Cluster polymer sequence** (Step 580): `IsClusterPolymerSequence G hn ω` for `ω : Fin n → polymers` with `n ≥ 1` — every entry is a polymer of `G` and the index-side incompatibility graph on `Fin n` is `Connected`. Sequence-level analogue of `IsClusterPolymerSet` allowing multiplicities (the same polymer may appear at multiple indices), as required by the Mayer-expansion sum. `IsClusterPolymerSequence.singleton`: any one-element sequence whose entry is a polymer is a cluster sequence. **Cluster-sequence activity** (Step 581): `clusterSeqActivity t ω := ∏ i, t ^ (ω i).card` — the activity factor multiplying the Ursell coefficient in the Mayer expansion `log Ξ = ∑_n (1/n!) ∑_ω ϕ^T(ω) · z(ω)`. API: singleton (`n=1`), non-negativity for `0 ≤ t`, empty sequence (`n=0`) equals `1`. **Connected spanning edge subsets** (Step 582): `connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets G : Finset (Finset (Sym2 V))` for finite-vertex `G` — edge subsets `S ⊆ G.edgeFinset` such that the spanning subgraph `SimpleGraph.fromEdgeSet ↑S` (with the same vertex set `V`) is `Connected`. API: `mem_connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets` (membership characterisation). **Ursell coefficient** (Step 583): `ursellCoefficient (ω : Fin n → polymers) : ℝ := (∑_{S ∈ connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets G(ω)} (-1)^{\|S\|}) / n!` — the combinatorial weight in the Mayer expansion `log Ξ = ∑_{n ≥ 1} ∑_ω ϕ^T(ω) · z(ω)`. Singleton case `ursellCoefficient_singleton`: `ϕ^T(ω) = 1` for `n = 1` (graph on `Fin 1` has no edges, only edge subset is `∅`, spanning is connected). **Vanishing for disconnected sequences** (Step 584): `ursellCoefficient_eq_zero_of_disconnected`: `ϕ^T(ω) = 0` whenever `G(ω)` is not `Connected`. The Mayer-expansion sum effectively restricts to cluster sequences (Step 580). Proof: any connected spanning subgraph `fromEdgeSet ↑S` of `G(ω)` implies `G(ω)` itself is `Connected` (via `Reachable.mono`), so disconnected `G(ω)` forces `connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets = ∅`. **Pair Ursell coefficient (incompatible)** (Step 585): `ursellCoefficient_pair_incompatible`: for `ω : Fin 2 → polymers` with `PolymersIncompatible (ω 0) (ω 1)`, `ϕ^T(ω) = -1/2`. **Pair Ursell coefficient (compatible)** (Step 586): `ursellCoefficient_pair_compatible`: for compatible pairs, `ϕ^T(ω) = 0` (Step 584 applies — Fin 2 graph with no edge is disconnected). **Unified** `ursellCoefficient_pair`: case-conditional formula `ϕ^T(ω) = if PolymersIncompatible (ω 0) (ω 1) then -1/2 else 0`. **Mayer expansion n-th term** (Step 587): `mayerExpansionTerm G n t := ∑_{ω ∈ piFinset (allPolymers G)} ϕ^T(ω) · z(t, ω)` — the contribution of `n`-element polymer sequences to `log Ξ`; the `1/n!` factor is already absorbed into `ursellCoefficient` (Step 583). `mayerExpansionTerm_zero`: `= 0` (empty sequence's Ursell vanishes by disconnectedness). `mayerExpansionTerm_one`: `= ∑_{P ∈ allPolymers G} t^|P|` (singleton ϕ^T = 1, z = t^|P|, reindex via `ω ↦ ω 0`). **Continuity in `t`** (Step 588): `clusterSeqActivity_continuous` (finite product of monomials) and `mayerExpansionTerm_continuous` (finite sum of constants times continuous functions). **Differentiability in `t`** (Step 589): `clusterSeqActivity_differentiable` and `mayerExpansionTerm_differentiable` strengthen continuity to `Differentiable ℝ` on all of `ℝ`, since each term is a polynomial. **Real-analyticity in `t`** (Step 590): `clusterSeqActivity_analyticAt`, `mayerExpansionTerm_analyticAt`, and global `mayerExpansionTerm_analyticOnNhd` over `Set.univ`. Each Mayer term is a polynomial in `t`, hence real-analytic at every point. **Mayer partial sum** (Step 591): `mayerPartialSum G N t := ∑_{n = 0..N} mayerExpansionTerm G n t` — finite truncation through cluster size `N`. Inherits `Continuous`, `Differentiable ℝ`, `AnalyticAt ℝ` (at every `t`), and global `AnalyticOnNhd ℝ _ Set.univ`. **Base cases** (Step 592): `mayerPartialSum_zero`: `= 0` at N=0. `mayerPartialSum_one`: `= ∑_{P ∈ allPolymers G} t^|P|` at N=1 (leading non-trivial truncation = total polymer activity). **n=2 explicit pair sum** (Step 593): `mayerExpansionTerm G 2 t = ∑_{(P, Q) ∈ allPolymers²} (if PolymersIncompatible P Q then -1/2 else 0) · t^|P| · t^|Q|`. Reindex via `Finset.sum_bij` between `piFinset` and `allPolymers ×ˢ allPolymers` (sending `ω ↦ (ω 0, ω 1)`); apply pair Ursell formula. **Lift to β/J via tanh chain** (Step 594): `mayerExpansionTerm_tanh_continuous_beta` / `_J` and `mayerPartialSum_tanh_continuous_beta` / `_J` — continuous in β (resp. J) with the other fixed, by composition with `Real.tanh (β·J)`. **Differentiable lift** (Step 595): `mayerExpansionTerm_tanh_differentiable_beta` / `_J` and `mayerPartialSum_tanh_differentiable_beta` / `_J` — same chain via `differentiable_real_tanh`. **AnalyticAt + AnalyticOnNhd lift** (Step 596): `mayerExpansionTerm_tanh_analyticAt_beta` / `_J`, `mayerPartialSum_tanh_analyticAt_beta` / `_J`, plus global `mayerPartialSum_tanh_analyticOnNhd_beta` / `_J` over `Set.univ`. Chain through `analyticAt_real_tanh` and analytic linear factor `β·J`. The Mayer truncation `mayerPartialSum G N` is now a real-analytic function of β/J on all of ℝ, mirroring `freeEnergy_analyticOnNhd` from §18.6. **n=2 filter form** (Step 597): `mayerExpansionTerm_two_filter`: `mayerExpansionTerm G 2 t = -1/2 · ∑_{(P, Q) ∈ allPolymers², PolymersIncompatible P Q} t^|P| · t^|Q|` — restricted to incompatible pairs. **Vanishing at t=0** (Step 598): `mayerExpansionTerm_at_zero` and `mayerPartialSum_at_zero` (`= 0` for all n / N). For n=0 via Step 587; for n ≥ 1 since every polymer has |P| ≥ 1, so `0 ^ |P| = 0` and `clusterSeqActivity 0 ω = 0`. **vdPolymerFamilies_sum at t=0** (Step 599): `vdPolymerFamilies_sum_at_zero`: `∑_{Γ ∈ vdCompatiblePolymerFamilies G} ∏_{P ∈ Γ} 0^|P| = 1` (only empty family `Γ = ∅` contributes via empty product; non-empty families contribute 0). **Mayer identity at t=0** (Step 600 milestone): `mayer_identity_at_zero`: `Real.log (vdPolymerFamilies_sum G 0) = mayerPartialSum G N 0` for any `N` (both sides = 0). First verified instance of the Mayer expansion identity `log Ξ = ∑_{n ≥ 0} mayerExpansionTerm G n t`; the general-`t` identity requires substantial Mayer/Ursell power-series manipulations (deferred). **Ursell absolute bound** (Step 601): `|ϕ^T(ω)| ≤ |connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets G(ω)| / n!` via triangle inequality on the alternating sum (each `(-1)^|S|` has `|·| = 1`). **`connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets` cardinality** (Step 602): `|connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets G| ≤ 2^|G.edgeFinset|` (filter ⊆ powerset). **Uniform Ursell bound** (Step 603): `ursellCoefficient_abs_le_pow_div_factorial`: `|ϕ^T(ω)| ≤ 2^|E(G(ω))| / n!` — classical Mayer-expansion uniform bound combining Steps 601, 602. **Mayer term triangle bound** (Step 604): `mayerExpansionTerm_abs_le`: `|mayerExpansionTerm G n t| ≤ ∑_ω |ϕ^T(ω)| · |z(t,ω)|` via `Finset.abs_sum_le_sum_abs` + `abs_mul`. **Generic positivity** (Step 605): `vdPolymerFamilies_sum_ge_one_of_nonneg`: `1 ≤ ∑ Γ ∏ t^|P|` for any `t ≥ 0` (empty family contributes 1, others non-negative); `vdPolymerFamilies_sum_pos_of_nonneg`: `> 0`. Strengthens Step 549 from the tanh form to a generic non-negative activity. **`log` analyticity** (Step 606): `log_vdPolymerFamilies_sum_analyticAt`: `Real.log (vdPolymerFamilies_sum G t)` is `AnalyticAt ℝ` at every `t ≥ 0`, via `AnalyticAt.log` of the analytic polymer-family sum (positive). **Global form** (Step 607): `log_vdPolymerFamilies_sum_analyticOnNhd_Ici_zero`: `AnalyticOnNhd ℝ _ (Set.Ici 0)` lifting Step 606. **`log` analyticity in β / J** (Step 608): `log_vdPolymerFamilies_sum_tanh_analyticAt_beta` / `_J` under `0 ≤ β·J` — `Real.log (∑_Γ ∏ tanh(β·J)^|P|)` is `AnalyticAt ℝ` in β (resp. J). Combines Step 562 (vdSum analytic in β/J via tanh) with positivity at `tanh(β·J) ≥ 0`. **Mayer identity at β·J=0** (Step 609): `mayer_identity_at_betaJ_zero` (general), `mayer_identity_at_beta_zero` (β=0), `mayer_identity_at_J_zero` (J=0) — extend Step 600 to β/J directions via `tanh 0 = 0`. **Polymer free energy** (Step 610): `polymerFreeEnergy G t := Real.log (∑_Γ ∏ t^|P|)` — named wrapper for the LHS of the Mayer identity. `polymerFreeEnergy_at_zero = 0`, `polymerFreeEnergy_analyticAt` for `t ≥ 0`, `polymerFreeEnergy_analyticOnNhd_Ici_zero` (global form over `Set.Ici 0`). **Continuity / differentiability + restated identity** (Step 611): `polymerFreeEnergy_continuousAt` and `_differentiableAt` (consequences of analyticAt); `polymerFreeEnergy_eq_mayerPartialSum_at_zero` (Mayer identity at t=0 in named-wrapper form). **freeEnergy decomposition** (Step 612): `freeEnergy_eq_polymerFreeEnergy`: under `0 < |ι|` and `0 ≤ β·J`, `freeEnergy G ⟨J, 0, β⟩ = log 2 + (|E|/|ι|) · log cosh(β·J) + polymerFreeEnergy G (tanh(β·J)) / |ι|`. Restatement of Step 317 in polymer-family form (via Step 547 bijection), connecting the actual Ising free energy to the polymer free energy. **β/J analyticity wrappers** (Step 613): `polymerFreeEnergy_tanh_analyticAt_beta` / `_J` (named-wrapper restatement of Step 608) and `_analyticOnNhd_beta_Ici_zero` / `_J_Ici_zero` (under `J ≥ 0` / `β ≥ 0` gives global form on `[0, ∞)`). **N=2 explicit Mayer truncation** (Step 614): `mayerPartialSum_two`: `mayerPartialSum G 2 t = ∑_P t^|P| - (1/2) ∑_{(P, Q) incompat} t^|P|·t^|Q|` — leading two-term Mayer truncation in fully explicit polymer-sum form. **Uniform Ursell bound** (Step 615): `ursellCoefficient_abs_le_choose_pow_div_factorial`: `|ϕ^T(ω)| ≤ 2^(n choose 2) / n!` — independent of `ω` (combines Step 603 with `SimpleGraph.card_edgeFinset_le_card_choose_two`). **Explicit convergence radius via `log` `HasSum`** (PR #1517): `polymerFreeEnergy_hasSum_via_log_of_pow_lt_two` — under `(1+t)^|E| < 2`, `polymerFreeEnergy G t = ∑_n …` as a `HasSum` of explicit terms; named convergence radius for the polymer expansion. **Mayer Phase B base cases via `decide`** (PRs #1518, #1519, #1542-#1546, #1553): explicit alternating connected-subgraph sums `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_K3 = 2`, `_K4 = -6` (PRs #1518/#1519, `set_option maxRecDepth 2000`), `pathGraph` for `n = 3..8` with alternating signs `1, -1, 1, -1, 1, -1` (PRs #1542-#1544; these per-`n` `decide` cases have since been **replaced** by the general path formula recorded below, which supplies the same values and additionally `pathGraph 1 = 1`, `pathGraph 2 = -1`), `cycleGraph` for `n = 3..7` with values `2, -3, 4, -5, 6` (PRs #1545, #1546, #1553). General `K_n` formula `(-1)^(n-1)·(n-1)!` is **proved** (`alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_closed_form`) via the **root-component recurrence** (no chromatic-polynomial / matrix-tree machinery needed); see the `D_n` foundation, iso-invariance, root-component split, reindex and closed-form entries below. The companion **general cycle formula** `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum (cycleGraph n) = (-1)^(n-1)·(n-1)` (for `n ≥ 3`, `ClusterExpansion/CycleGraphAlternatingSum.lean`, `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_cycleGraph`) is proved by the explicit characterization that the connected spanning subgraphs of `C_n` are exactly the full cycle (size `n`) plus the `n` single-edge deletions (Hamiltonian paths, size `n-1`): the forward bound uses `Connected.card_vert_le_card_edgeSet_add_one`, and each edge-deletion is shown connected by the rotation automorphism (`circulantGraph_adj_translate`) reducing it to the wrap edge, where `pathGraph_connected.mono` applies. This **replaces** the former per-`n` `decide` cases (`alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_cycleGraph_{three,four,five,six,seven}`), eliminating the 4M-heartbeat cycle-7 `decide` (~280s clean-build cost; now ~7s). The companion **general path formula** `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum (pathGraph (n+1)) = (-1)^n` (all `n`, `ClusterExpansion/PathGraphAlternatingSum.lean`, `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_pathGraph`) is proved by a pure edge count with no tree/bridge theory: a connected spanning subgraph on `n+1` vertices needs at least `n` edges (`Connected.card_vert_le_card_edgeSet_add_one`) and the path has exactly `n` (`card_pathGraph_edgeFinset`), so the two bounds squeeze and the full edge set is the unique connected spanning subset (`pathGraph_connectedSpanning_charac`), leaving the single term `(-1)^n`. This **replaces** the former per-`n` `decide +kernel` cases (`alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_pathGraph_{three,...,eight}`), removing the `maxHeartbeats 400000` escalation at `pathGraph 8` (module cost 38s to 2s) and extending the coverage from `n = 3..8` vertices to all `n`. **Filter forms** (PRs #1521, #1522): `mayerExpansionTerm_filter_connected` and `mayerPartialSum_filter_connected` — restrict the Mayer sums to connected polymer-sequence supports without changing the value. **`polymerFreeEnergy` upper bounds** (PRs #1523, #1524, #1525): `polymerFreeEnergy_le_eps_of_nonneg`, `_le_pow_sub_one_of_nonneg`, `_lt_log_two_of_pow_lt_two`, plus tanh-substituted forms. **High-temperature sandwich** (PR #1526): `polymerFreeEnergy_high_temp_sandwich` (5-tuple) and **`freeEnergy_lt_log_two_plus_high_temp_correction`** (PR #1527). **Strict / iff characterisations** (PRs #1547-#1550, #1554-#1560): `polymerFreeEnergy_lt_eps_of_eps_pos` (strict; PR #1547), `_eq_zero_iff_eps_eq_zero` and `_pos_iff_eps_pos` (PR #1548), `_lt_eps_iff_eps_pos` (PR #1549), tanh-substituted strict / iff forms (PR #1550), `polymerFreeEnergy_lt_pow_sub_one_of_eps_pos` and tanh form (PRs #1554, #1555), `mayerExpansionTerm_eq_zero_of_no_polymers` (PR #1556), `vdPolymerFamilies_sum_eq_one_iff_eps_eq_zero` and `_gt_one_iff_eps_pos` (PR #1557), bundle of ε > 0 / = 0 iff polymers + tanh forms (10 thm; PR #1558), strict-mono bundle (4 thm; PR #1559), strict-pos bundle + `StrictMonoOn (Set.Ioi 0)` (8 thm; PR #1560). **Pair connectivity / filter** (PRs #1551, #1552): `polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph_two_connected_iff_incompatible` and `mayerExpansionTerm_two_filter_connected_eq_incompat`. **`mayerPartialSum` strict-pos / sign / N=0** (PR #1562; 3 thm). **Λ-layer wrappers** (PRs #1563, #1564): 10 + 8 theorems in `AmbientLattice/Analyticity.lean` lifting the abstract API to the Λ layer. **Ferromagnetic forms** (PR #1565; 9 thm) — `0 ≤ J, 0 < β` versions of the strict / iff bundle via `mul_nonneg`. **tanh-monotonicity in β / J** (PR #1566; 5 thm + local helper `real_tanh_strictMono`) — `polymerFreeEnergy (tanh(β·J))` is monotone (resp. strictly monotone) in β and J under the appropriate ferromagnetic sign assumptions. (Steps 503-548, 576-615; PRs #1517-#1566; Issue #1344.) **Mayer Phase B `D_n` foundation** (`ClusterExpansion/AlternatingCompleteGraph.lean` (declaration-free facade; the declarations live in `AlternatingCompleteGraph/{SignedSums,CompleteGraphSmallCases,CompleteGraphK4,MayerConnectedFilter}.lean`), Part of #1499): toward the general-`n` Mayer coefficient identity `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum K_n = (-1)^{n-1}(n-1)!` (base cases `n ≤ 4` already done) via the root-component recurrence `D_n = ∑_{C ∋ 0} c_{|C|} D_{n-|C|}` (`c = alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum`, `D = allSignedSubgraphSum` the signed sum over *all* spanning edge-subsets). `allSignedSubgraphSum_eq_zero_of_edgeFinset_nonempty` (`D = 0` when there is an edge, via `Finset.sum_powerset_neg_one_pow_card_of_nonempty`), `_eq_one_of_edgeFinset_empty` (`D = 1` edgeless), `allSignedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_eq_zero_of_two_le` (`D_n = 0`, `n ≥ 2`) and `_eq_one_of_subsingleton` (`D_n = 1`, `n ≤ 1`) establish the `D_n` boundary values for the recurrence. **Iso-invariance** `allSignedSubgraphSum_iso` and `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_iso` (for `φ : G ≃g H`, `D`/`c` are graph-isomorphism invariants): the edge-relabelling `S ↦ S.map (Sym2.map φ)` is a cardinality-preserving (and, for `c`, connectivity-preserving via `SimpleGraph.Iso.connected_iff`) bijection on edge-subsets. Supplies the `c_{|C|} = c(K_{|C|})`, `D_{n-|C|} = D(K_{n-|C|})` ingredients of the recurrence via `K_n|_C ≅ K_{|C|}`. **Bijection foundations**: `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_card` / `allSignedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_card` (`c`/`D` of `K_V` depend only on `|V|`, iso-invariance applied to `K_V ≅ K_{Fin |V|}`); `connectedComponentMk_eq_of_mem` (edges of `S` do not cross components of `fromEdgeSet ↑S`, via `connectedComponentMk_eq_of_adj` — the crossing-edge-free property underlying the root-component split). **Root-component vertex set** (`ClusterExpansion/MayerRootComponent.lean` (declaration-free facade; the declarations live in `MayerRootComponent/{ComponentFiber,FiberProduct,FactorReindex,RecurrenceClosedForm,UrsellComplete}.lean`)): `rootComponentFinset S r` = vertices in the connected component of `r` in `fromEdgeSet ↑S`; `mem_rootComponentFinset`, `self_mem_rootComponentFinset` (`r ∈` its own component), and `mem_rootComponentFinset_iff_of_mem_edge` (for `s(a,b) ∈ S`, `a ∈ C ↔ b ∈ C` — both endpoints fall in the root component together), the set-level crossing-edge-free property for the `D_n = ∑_{C ∋ 0} c_{|C|} D_{n-|C|}` split. **Edge-count split** `rootComponent_edge_card_split` (`#(S∩C.sym2) + #(S∩Cᶜ.sym2) = #S` via `Finset.sym2` + `Finset.card_filter_add_card_filter_not` + crossing-free). **Inside-connected crux** `induce_fromEdgeSet_inside_connected` (the within-`C` edges `S ∩ C.sym2` induce a *connected* graph on `C`): since `↑C = (fromEdgeSet ↑S).connectedComponentMk r).supp` (`coe_rootComponentFinset`) and the inside edges induce the same graph on `C` as `S` (`induce_fromEdgeSet_inside_eq`), the induced graph is the component's `toSimpleGraph`, connected by `SimpleGraph.ConnectedComponent.connected_toSimpleGraph`. The hardest ingredient of the multiplicative split — that `S_in` is connected-spanning on `C`. **Root-component fibre product split** (`MayerRootComponent.lean`): `reachable_stays_in_of_no_cross` (with no crossing edge, reachability from the root stays inside `C`, via `Relation.ReflTransGen` induction on the reachability witness); `rootComponentFinset_eq_iff` (the fibre characterisation `rootComponentFinset S r = C ↔ r ∈ C ∧ (no edge of S crosses C) ∧ (S ∩ C.sym2 induces a connected graph on C)`, packaging `induce_fromEdgeSet_inside_connected` forwards and `reachable_stays_in_of_no_cross` backwards); `mem_sym2_and_compl_sym2_false` (`C.sym2` and `Cᶜ.sym2` are disjoint); the inside/outside ambient signed-subgraph sets `insideConnectedEdgeSubsets`, `outsideEdgeSubsets` with their membership lemmas; and the **lemma-6 capstone** `fiber_signed_sum_eq_product`: `∑_{S : rootComponentFinset S r = C} (-1)^|S| = (∑_{A ∈ inside} (-1)^|A|) · (∑_{B ∈ outside} (-1)^|B|)`, proved by the crossing-free bijection `S ↦ (S ∩ C.sym2, S ∩ Cᶜ.sym2)` with inverse `(A, B) ↦ A ∪ B` (`Finset.sum_bij'`), with `rootComponent_edge_card_split` giving `|S| = |A| + |B|` so `(-1)^|S| = (-1)^|A|·(-1)^|B|`. The ambient multiplicative core of the recurrence `D_n = ∑_{C ∋ 0} c_{|C|} D_{n-|C|}` (GJ §18.4). Still **Partial**: the fibrewise summation over `C` with the reindex `inside ≅ c(K_C)`, `outside ≅ D(K_{Cᶜ})` collapsing to the closed form `(-1)^{n-1}(n-1)!` is done (the general `K_n` formula below); the remaining gap is the general-`t` Mayer expansion identity (Issue #1499). **Fibrewise recurrence + outside factor** (`MayerRootComponent.lean`): `real_signed_sum_powerset` (`∑_{B ⊆ X} (-1)^|B| = if X = ∅ then 1 else 0`, real-cast of `Finset.sum_powerset_neg_one_pow_card`); `allSignedSubgraphSum_eq_ite` (`D(G) = if G.edgeFinset = ∅ then 1 else 0`); the **lemma-7 fibrewise sum** `allSignedSubgraphSum_eq_sum_fiber_product`: `D(G) = ∑_{C : r ∈ C} insideΣ(C)·outsideΣ(C)` via `Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to` on the root-component map `S ↦ rootComponentFinset S r` together with the per-fibre product split; and the outside-factor evaluation `outsideEdgeSubsets_eq_powerset` (the outside subsets are exactly the powerset of `G.edgeFinset ∩ Cᶜ.sym2`) + `outsideEdgeSubsets_signed_sum_eq_ite` (`outsideΣ(C) = if G.edgeFinset ∩ Cᶜ.sym2 = ∅ then 1 else 0`). The ambient recurrence `D_n = ∑_{C ∋ 0} insideΣ(C)·outsideΣ(C)` is now in place; the remaining reindex identifies `insideΣ(C) = c(K_C)`, `outsideΣ(C) = D(K_{Cᶜ})` (subtype complete graphs) and collapses the recurrence to `c_n + (n-1)c_{n-1} = 0`. **Outside factor reindex** (`MayerRootComponent.lean`, outside half of lemma 8): `completeGraph_edgeFinset_inter_compl_sym2_empty_iff` (`(⊤).edgeFinset ∩ Cᶜ.sym2 = ∅ ↔ Cᶜ.card ≤ 1` — an edge inside `Cᶜ` needs two distinct complement vertices); `allSignedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_subtype_eq_ite` (`D(K_C) = if C.card ≤ 1 then 1 else 0`, via `allSignedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_card` → `Fin` boundary lemmas); and `outsideEdgeSubsets_completeGraph_signed_sum`: `outsideΣ(C) = allSignedSubgraphSum (⊤ : SimpleGraph ↑Cᶜ) = D(K_{Cᶜ})` (both sides reduce to `if Cᶜ.card ≤ 1 then 1 else 0`, avoiding subtype edge-set rewrites). The remaining inside reindex `insideΣ(C) = c(K_C)` (connectivity-preserving bijection) and the recurrence collapse follow. **Inside factor reindex + root-component recurrence** (`MayerRootComponent.lean`, inside half of lemma 8): the graph-equality crux `induce_fromEdgeSet_map_subtype` (`(fromEdgeSet ↑(T.map e)).induce ↑C = fromEdgeSet ↑T` on `↑C`, transferring connectivity through the subtype `sym2`-embedding `e`); the roundtrip helpers `inside_mem_range_sym2Map` (every inside edge is `e z`) and `inside_preimage_map_eq` (`(A.preimage e).map e = A`); the inside reindex `insideConnectedEdgeSubsets_completeGraph_signed_sum`: `insideΣ(C) = alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum (⊤ : SimpleGraph ↑C) = c(K_C)` (connectivity-preserving `Finset.sum_bij'` `T ↦ T.map e` / `A ↦ A.preimage e`, `Finset.preimage_map`); and the **capstone** `allSignedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_root_recurrence`: `D_n = ∑_{C ∋ r} c(K_C)·D(K_{Cᶜ})` — the full root-component recurrence `D_n = ∑_{C ∋ 0} c_{|C|} D_{n-|C|}`, assembling lemma 7 with both reindexes. **Recurrence collapse and closed form** (`MayerRootComponent.lean`, completing the programme): `mayer_surviving_set` (the `C ∋ 0` with `|Cᶜ| ≤ 1` are exactly `univ` and the `n-1` cofinite singletons `{j}ᶜ`, `j ≠ 0`); `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_recurrence` (`c_n + (n-1)·c_{n-1} = 0` for `n ≥ 2`, collapsing the root-component recurrence via `D(K_{Cᶜ}) = 0` unless `|Cᶜ| ≤ 1`, cardinality-invariance of `c`, and `D_n = 0`); and the **capstone** `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_completeGraph_closed_form`: `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum K_n = (-1)^(n-1)·(n-1)!` for `n ≥ 1` (induction from the recurrence, base `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_K1`). This is the general-`n` Mayer/Ursell coefficient identity — the root-component recurrence route avoids the chromatic-polynomial / matrix-tree machinery entirely. **Done.** **Ursell coefficient of a fully-incompatible cluster** (`MayerRootComponent.lean`): `ursellCoefficient_eq_alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_div` (`ϕ^T(ω) = c(G(ω))/n!`); `polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph_eq_top_of_pairwise` (all pairs incompatible ⟹ `G(ω) = ⊤`); `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_polymerSeq_complete` (the complete cluster's connected-spanning sum is `(-1)^(n-1)(n-1)!`, via the identity iso `G(ω) ≃g ⊤` and the closed form); and `ursellCoefficient_complete` / `ursellCoefficient_complete_eq`: the classic single-cluster Mayer value `ϕ^T(ω) = (-1)^(n-1)(n-1)!/n! = (-1)^(n-1)/n` for `n` pairwise incompatible polymers — connecting the K_n closed form to the actual Ursell coefficient of the cluster expansion. **Complete-cluster Mayer contribution** (`MayerCompleteContribution.lean`): `ursellCoefficient_complete_abs_eq` (`|ϕ^T(ω)| = 1/n`); `ursellCoefficient_complete_eq_two` (`n = 2` recovers `-1/2`, consistent with `ursellCoefficient_pair_incompatible`); and `mayerExpansionTerm_completeClusterSubsum_eq` (the part of the Mayer term over fully-incompatible sequences factors as `((-1)^(n-1)/n)·∑ clusterSeqActivity`, the constant Ursell coefficient pulled out of the activity sum). **Single-polymer cluster contribution** (`MayerCompleteContribution.lean`): `clusterSeqActivity_const` (a repeated polymer's activity is `(t^|P|)^m`) and the capstone `hasSum_singlePolymer_ursell_eq_log` / `tsum_singlePolymer_ursell_eq_log`: `∑_{m≥1} ϕ^T(P,…,P)·(t^|P|)^m = log(1 + t^|P|)` for `|t^|P|| < 1` — the classic identity that a single polymer contributes `log(1 + activity)` to `log Ξ`, derived from the Ursell closed form `(-1)^(m-1)/m` (a repeated polymer is a self-incompatible complete cluster) and the logarithm power series `hasSum_pow_div_log_of_abs_lt_one`. This is the log structure at the heart of why the cluster expansion exponentiates. **Single-polymer convergence** (GJ §18.5, `MayerCompleteContribution.lean`): `singlePolymer_ursell_term_eq` (the repeated-polymer term in closed form `-((-(t^|P|))^{m+1}/(m+1))`); `hasSum_abs_singlePolymer_ursell` / `tsum_abs_singlePolymer_ursell` (absolute convergence: `∑_m |ϕ^T(P,…,P)·z| = ∑_m z^{m+1}/(m+1) = -log(1 - t^|P|)` for `0 ≤ t`, `t^|P| < 1` — the explicit convergence radius); `summable_singlePolymer_ursell`; and `singlePolymer_log_le_neg_log` (`log(1+z) ≤ -log(1-z)` for `0 ≤ z < 1`, the contribution dominated by its absolute-convergence value). **Independent-polymer upper bound** (GJ §18.5, `MayerCompleteContribution.lean`): `vdPolymerFamilies_sum_le_prod_one_add` (`∑_Γ ∏_{P∈Γ} t^|P| ≤ ∏_P (1 + t^|P|)` for `t ≥ 0`, compatible families being a subset of all subsets, `Finset.prod_one_add`); `polymerFreeEnergy_le_sum_log_one_add` (`polymerFreeEnergy G t ≤ ∑_P log(1 + t^|P|)` via `Real.log` monotonicity and `Real.log_prod`); and `polymerFreeEnergy_le_sum_singlePolymer_contribution` (in the regime `t^|P| < 1`, the free energy is dominated by the sum of independent single-polymer Mayer contributions `∑_P ∑'_m ϕ^T(P,…,P)·z`). The polymer free energy bounded by the independent single-polymer contributions, a sharp convergence bound. **Independent-polymer lower bound and sandwich** (GJ §18.5, `MayerCompleteContribution.lean`): `IsCompatiblePolymerFamilyVertexDisjoint.singleton` (a single polymer is a compatible family); `one_add_sum_le_vdPolymerFamilies_sum` (`1 + ∑_P t^|P| ≤ ∑_Γ ∏_{P∈Γ} t^|P|`, the empty and single-polymer families contributing `1` and `∑_P t^|P|`); `log_one_add_sum_le_polymerFreeEnergy` (`log(1 + ∑_P t^|P|) ≤ polymerFreeEnergy G t`); and the bundled `polymerFreeEnergy_sandwich_independent`: `log(1 + ∑_P t^|P|) ≤ polymerFreeEnergy G t ≤ ∑_P log(1 + t^|P|)` — the free energy bracketed by the two independent-polymer expressions. **Ising free energy sandwich** (GJ §18.5, `MayerCompleteContribution.lean`): `freeEnergy_sandwich_independent_polymer` substitutes the sandwich into the polymer decomposition (`freeEnergy_eq_polymerFreeEnergy`) to bracket the physical free energy at activity `tanh(β·J)`: `log 2 + (|E|/|ι|)·log cosh(β·J) + log(1 + ∑_P tanh(β·J)^|P|)/|ι| ≤ freeEnergy G ⟨J,0,β⟩ ≤ … + (∑_P log(1 + tanh(β·J)^|P|))/|ι|` (for `0 ≤ β·J`, `0 < |ι|`); and `freeEnergy_le_log_two_plus_sum_tanh_pow` gives the coarser explicit upper bound `freeEnergy ≤ log 2 + (|E|/|ι|)·log cosh(β·J) + (∑_P tanh(β·J)^|P|)/|ι|` via `log(1+x) ≤ x`. **Independent (non-interacting) polymer free energy** (GJ §18.5, `MayerCore/IndependentPolymer.lean`): when the polymers are pairwise vertex-disjoint (`(allPolymers G).Pairwise IsPolymerVertexDisjoint`), every subset is a compatible family (`vdCompatiblePolymerFamilies_eq_powerset_of_pairwise_disjoint`), so the partition function factorises `∑_Γ ∏_{P∈Γ} t^|P| = ∏_P (1 + t^|P|)` (`vdPolymerFamilies_sum_eq_prod_of_pairwise_disjoint`), the polymer free energy is the single-polymer log sum `polymerFreeEnergy G t = ∑_P log(1+t^|P|)` (`polymerFreeEnergy_eq_sum_log_of_pairwise_disjoint`, `t ≥ 0`), and the Ising free energy has the closed form `f = log 2 + (|E|/|ι|)·log cosh(βJ) + (∑_P log(1+tanh(βJ)^|P|))/|ι|` (`freeEnergy_eq_sum_log_of_pairwise_disjoint`) — the exactly-solvable non-interacting case of the cluster expansion. **Mixed-cluster vanishing** (`MayerCore/IndependentVanishing.lean`): for a non-interacting gas the `n`-th Mayer term collapses to the diagonal — `ursellCoefficient_eq_zero_of_pairwise_compatible_not_constant` (a non-constant sequence of pairwise-compatible polymers has a disconnected incompatibility graph, since adjacency forces equal values, so `ϕ^T(ω) = 0`) and `mayerExpansionTerm_eq_sum_diagonal_of_pairwise_compatible` (`mayerExpansionTerm G n t = ∑_P ϕ^T(P,…,P)·(t^|P|)^n` for `n ≥ 1`), reducing the Mayer expansion to the single-polymer multiplicity series — the cluster-expansion side of the independent free energy. **Non-interacting Mayer capstone** (`MayerCore/IndependentCapstone.lean`): for pairwise vertex-disjoint polymers, `0 ≤ t`, and `t^|P| < 1` for all `P`, the Mayer expansion converges to the free energy — `hasSum_mayerExpansionTerm_of_pairwise_disjoint` (`HasSum (fun n => mayerExpansionTerm G n t) (polymerFreeEnergy G t)`) and `polymerFreeEnergy_eq_tsum_mayerExpansionTerm_of_pairwise_disjoint` (`polymerFreeEnergy G t = ∑'_n mayerExpansionTerm G n t`), combining the diagonal collapse, the single-polymer series `∑_m ϕ^T(P,…,P)·(t^|P|)^m = log(1+t^|P|)` (`hasSum_singlePolymer_ursell_eq_log` + `hasSum_nat_add_iff`), and the finite polymer sum (`hasSum_sum`). This is the cluster-expansion capstone `log Ξ = ∑_n` cluster terms, verified for the exactly-solvable non-interacting regime (the general interacting case requires Kotecky–Preiss). **Ising free energy as a Mayer expansion** (`MayerCore/IndependentFreeEnergyMayer.lean`): combining the polymer decomposition with the capstone, for pairwise vertex-disjoint polymers, `0 ≤ βJ`, `0 < |ι|`, `freeEnergy G ⟨J,0,β⟩ = log 2 + (|E|/|ι|)·log cosh(βJ) + (∑'_n mayerExpansionTerm G n (tanh βJ))/|ι|` (`freeEnergy_eq_tsum_mayer_of_pairwise_disjoint`, + ferromagnetic form) — the physical free energy expressed through its convergent cluster expansion; the convergence `tanh(βJ)^|P| < 1` is automatic since polymers are non-empty and `tanh < 1` (`tanh_pow_lt_one_of_mem_allPolymers`). **Three-vertex alternating-sum classification** (`AlternatingFinThree.lean`): the path values `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_fin_three_path_{01_12,01_02,02_12}` (`= 1`, the only connected spanning subset of a 2-edge graph on `Fin 3` is the full pair, `(-1)^2 = 1`, proved by `decide` on `connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets`) complete the 3-vertex classification alongside the triangle `alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_K3 = 2` and the disconnected `= 0` cases — these are the `n = 3` Mayer/Ursell numerators (`ϕ^T = 1/3! = 1/6` for a path-shaped incompatibility cluster, `1/3` for the fully-incompatible triangle). **The n = 3 Ursell coefficient by pattern** (`UrsellFinThree.lean`): `ursellCoefficient_fin_three_path_{01_12,01_02,02_12} = 1/6` — when exactly two of the three pairs of `ω : Fin 3 → polymers` are incompatible (path-shaped incompatibility graph), via the identity isomorphism `polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph ω ≃g fromEdgeSet {path edges}` (`alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_iso` + the path values) + `ursellCoefficient_eq_alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_div`. The remaining patterns are packaged as per-`ω` lemmas in the same file: `ursellCoefficient_fin_three_triangle` (all three pairs incompatible — complete graph `K_3`, `= 1/3` via `ursellCoefficient_complete_eq`), `ursellCoefficient_fin_three_no_edge` (all pairs compatible — edgeless graph, `= 0`), and `ursellCoefficient_fin_three_edge_{01,02,12}` (exactly one incompatible pair — a single edge with one isolated vertex, `= 0`); the four zero cases use the identity isomorphism to `fromEdgeSet {∅ / single edge}` followed by `SimpleGraph.Iso.connected_iff` and `decide`-disconnectedness, then `ursellCoefficient_eq_zero_of_disconnected`. Together with the path lemmas these eight lemmas give the complete explicit `n = 3` Ursell-coefficient classification by incompatibility pattern. **Closed form of the Mayer term for a non-interacting gas** (`MayerCore/IndependentMayerTerm.lean`): `ursellCoefficient_const_eq_of_isPolymer` (a constant sequence `(P,…,P)` of a nonempty polymer is fully incompatible, so `ϕ^T(P,…,P) = (-1)^(n-1)/n` via `PolymersIncompatible.self_of_isPolymer` + `ursellCoefficient_complete_eq`); `mayerExpansionTerm_eq_of_pairwise_disjoint`: for pairwise-compatible (vertex-disjoint) polymers and `n ≥ 1`, `mayerExpansionTerm G n t = ((-1)^(n-1)/n)·∑_P (t^|P|)^n` (diagonal collapse `mayerExpansionTerm_eq_sum_diagonal_of_pairwise_compatible` + constant Ursell value factored out of the sum); the specialisations `mayerExpansionTerm_{one,two,three}_eq_of_pairwise_disjoint` give the first Mayer coefficients `∑_P t^|P|`, `-½∑_P (t^|P|)²`, `⅓∑_P (t^|P|)³` (the `n = 3` value `1/3` matches the triangle Ursell value, the non-interacting analogue of `ursellCoefficient_fin_three_*`). **Unified n=3 Ursell classification** (`UrsellFinThree.lean`): `ursellCoefficient_fin_three_eq` packages all eight per-pattern lemmas into one statement — `ϕ^T(ω)` as a nested `if` on the three pair-incompatibility flags (`1/3` triangle, `1/6` path, `0` otherwise). **Penrose tree-graph inequality — unconditional foundations** (`ClusterExpansion/Penrose/BooleanInterval.lean`, `Penrose/SpanningTree.lean`, Issue #3954): the from-scratch route to a key remaining cluster-expansion-convergence input `\|alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum G\| ≤ numSpanningTrees G` (the partition-scheme construction and Kotecký–Preiss tree-sum induction still remain), rebuilt with **no hypothesis structures** (the earlier `PenrosePartitionScheme`-reduction chain was deleted as a missing-theorem seed). `booleanInterval lo hi := {S \| lo ⊆ S ⊆ hi}` with `mem_booleanInterval`, `booleanInterval_eq_empty_of_not_subset`, the addable-part parametrisation `booleanInterval_eq_image_powerset_sdiff` (`= (hi \ lo).powerset.image (lo ∪ ·)`), `disjoint_lo_of_subset_sdiff`, `card_union_of_subset_sdiff`, `union_lo_inj`, and the **Boolean-interval signed-sum cancellation** `sum_booleanInterval_neg_one_pow_card_int` / `_real` (`∑_{lo ⊆ S ⊆ hi} (-1)^{\|S\|} = (-1)^{\|lo\|}·∑_{U ⊆ hi∖lo}(-1)^{\|U\|}`), hence `_of_sdiff_nonempty` (`= 0` when `hi∖lo` nonempty) and `_of_eq` (survivor `(-1)^{\|lo\|}` when `hi = lo`) in both `ℤ` and `ℝ` — the sign-cancellation that drives the Penrose collapse, via `Finset.sum_powerset_neg_one_pow_card`. `spanningTreeEdgeSubsets G` (connected spanning edge-subsets with `\|V\|-1` edges), `numSpanningTrees G`, `mem_spanningTreeEdgeSubsets`, `spanningTreeEdgeSubsets_subset_connectedSpanning`, `_subset_powerset`, `numSpanningTrees_le_connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets_card`, `numSpanningTrees_le_two_pow`, and `numSpanningTrees_mono` (monotone in the graph). **Partition scheme — `treeOf` / `addable`** (`Penrose/PartitionScheme.lean`): the Penrose partition-scheme *data*, built by the edge-order Kruskal / reachability construction (no hypothesis structures). `edgeKey : Sym2 V → V ×ₗ V` (a strict-*total*-order key for edges via `Sym2.sortEquiv` into the lexicographic ordered pairs, with `edgeKey_injective` — **essential because mathlib's ambient `SetLike` `PartialOrder (Sym2 V)` is only partial, never comparing distinct edges**); `liftSym2Prop` (a symmetric vertex relation descends to `Sym2 V`, with `liftSym2Prop_mk`); `reachableLT X e` (the Kruskal already-connected test: `e`'s endpoints are joined within `fromEdgeSet` of the edges `f` of `X` with `edgeKey f < edgeKey e`); `treeOf S := S.filter (¬ reachableLT S ·)` (the Kruskal minimum spanning forest of `S`, keeping `e` iff its endpoints are not yet joined by the strictly-smaller edges of `S`); `addable G T := G.edgeFinset.filter (· ∉ T ∧ reachableLT T ·)` (the non-`T` edges of `G` whose endpoints are joined by strictly-smaller `T`-edges — the maximum edge of the fundamental cycle it forms with `T`, freely addable inside `T`'s Boolean interval). Structural lemmas: `mem_treeOf`, `treeOf_subset` (`treeOf S ⊆ S`), `treeOf_subset_edgeFinset`, `mem_addable`, `addable_subset_edges`, `addable_disjoint` (`Disjoint T (addable G T)`). The Kruskal correctness (`treeOf S` is a spanning tree) and the interval-partition property `treeOf S = T ↔ T ⊆ S ⊆ T ∪ addable G T` follow in later PRs. **Kruskal connectivity** (`Penrose/KruskalConnected.lean`): `treeOf` preserves reachability, hence connectivity. `edgesLT X e` (edges `f` of `X` with `edgeKey f < edgeKey e`) with `mem_edgesLT`, `reachableLT_iff_edgesLT`; `reachable_mono_of_edges_reachable` (transport via `reachable_iff_reflTransGen` + `ReflTransGen` induction); the Kruskal key lemma `reachable_endpoints_of_mem_treeOf` (every edge of `S` has `treeOf S`-reachable endpoints, by `WellFounded.induction` over the `edgeKey`-pullback well-founded edge order); the global equivalence `reachable_treeOf_iff_reachable`; `connected_treeOf_of_connected`; and `edgeFinset_fromEdgeSet_treeOf`. **Kruskal acyclicity + headline** (`Penrose/KruskalAcyclic.lean`): `treeOf S` is acyclic by the cycle-max argument — `cycle_edgeFinset_nonempty`, `reachable_from_cycle_edgeFinset_erase` (a cycle edge's endpoints are reachable via the other cycle edges, by localising to `fromEdgeSet ↑c.edges.toFinset` and `adj_and_reachable_delete_edges_iff_exists_cycle`), `cycle_edge_erase_subset_edgesLT` (non-maximal cycle edges are `edgesLT S M`), `isAcyclic_fromEdgeSet_treeOf` (a maximum-`edgeKey` cycle edge `M` would satisfy `reachableLT S M`, contradicting `M ∈ treeOf S`); hence `treeOf_card_eq_card_vertices_sub_one` (`|treeOf S| = |V|-1` via `IsTree.card_edgeFinset`) and the **headline** `treeOf_mem_spanningTreeEdgeSubsets` (`S ∈ connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets G → treeOf S ∈ spanningTreeEdgeSubsets G`). This PR also **fixed a latent order bug**: the earlier `<`-based `treeOf`/`edgesLT`/`reachableLT` silently resolved to mathlib's partial `SetLike` order on `Sym2 V` (under which distinct non-diagonal graph edges never compare, making the Kruskal test vacuous); they now use the explicit total `edgeKey` comparison. The remaining steps (interval property `treeOf S = T ↔ T ⊆ S ⊆ T ∪ addable G T` and Kotecký–Preiss tree-sum induction) build on this. **Interval-partition property** (`Penrose/IntervalPartition.lean`): the `treeOf` retraction partitions the connected spanning edge-subsets into Boolean intervals. New ingredient — the **prefix reachability equivalence** `reachableLT (treeOf S) e ↔ reachableLT S e` (`reachable_edgesLT_treeOf_of_mem_edgesLT` by `WellFounded.induction` over `edgeKey`, `reachable_edgesLT_treeOf_iff`, `reachableLT_treeOf_iff`), plus `edgesLT_mono`, `edgesLT_of_lt_subset`, `reachableLT_mono`. With `isTree_fromEdgeSet_of_mem_spanningTreeEdgeSubsets` and `not_reachableLT_of_mem_spanningTreeEdgeSubsets` (a tree edge is a bridge, hence not `reachableLT`): the forward `subset_union_addable_of_treeOf_eq`, the converse `treeOf_eq_of_subset_union_addable` (`not_reachableLT_of_interval_tree_edge` + `reachableLT_of_mem_addable_of_subset`), the **interval property** `treeOf_eq_iff_subset_union_addable` (`treeOf S = T ↔ T ⊆ S ∧ S ⊆ T ∪ addable G T`), and the **fiber identity** `treeOf_fiber_eq_booleanInterval` (`{S ∈ connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets G | treeOf S = T} = booleanInterval T (T ∪ addable G T)`). **Penrose tree-graph inequality** (`Penrose/TreeGraphBound.lean`, **milestone M1 complete**): `abs_alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum_le_numSpanningTrees : |alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum G| ≤ numSpanningTrees G`, unconditionally. Summing fiberwise over `treeOf` (`Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to` + the fiber identity) and the Boolean-interval cancellation: each fiber contributes `0` (addable part nonempty, via `(T ∪ addable G T) \ T = addable G T`) or `±1` (`abs_sum_booleanInterval_addable_le_one`), so the signed sum is bounded by the spanning-tree count. The Kotecký–Preiss tree-sum induction builds on this. **Ursell tree bound** (`ClusterExpansion/UrsellTreeBound.lean`, entry of milestone M2): lifting the Penrose inequality to the Ursell coefficient, `ursellCoefficient_abs_le_numSpanningTrees_div_factorial` (`|ϕ^T(ω)| ≤ numSpanningTrees (polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph ω) / n!`) and the uniform form `ursellCoefficient_abs_le_numSpanningTrees_top_div_factorial` (`≤ numSpanningTrees (⊤ : SimpleGraph (Fin n)) / n!`, via `Penrose.numSpanningTrees_mono` + `le_top`) — the sharp replacement of the trivial `2^|E|/n!` bound, feeding the convergence majorant. **Complete-graph spanning-tree count bound** (`Penrose/CompleteGraphTreeBound.lean`, milestone M2): `numSpanningTrees_top_fin_le_pow_pred` (`numSpanningTrees (⊤ : SimpleGraph (Fin n)) ≤ n^(n-1)`, unconditional) with its `Fin (n+1)` form `numSpanningTrees_top_fin_succ_le_pow` (`≤ (n+1)^n`), proved by a **parent-function injection** avoiding Prüfer/Cayley: `treeParent` (the unique neighbour of a non-root vertex decreasing its distance to the root by one, packaged by `existsUnique_parent_adj_dist_of_isTree` = `exists_parent_adj_dist_of_isTree` + `parent_adj_dist_unique_of_isTree`, using `IsTree.existsUnique_path` and the shortest-walk second vertex), the parent code `completeGraphTreeParentCode : Fin n → Fin (n+1)`, the **edge-set recovery** `completeGraphTree_edges_eq_parentCode_image` (the edge set of a spanning tree of `K_{n+1}` is exactly the image of its parent code — each tree edge is the parent edge of its endpoint farther from the root, by `IsTree.dist_eq_dist_add_one_of_adj`), hence `completeGraphTreeParentCode_injective` and the count bound via `Fintype.card_le_of_injective` + `Fintype.card_fun`. The weaker `n^(n-1)` (not Cayley's exact `n^(n-2)`) already yields the Mayer-series radius `1/e`, since `∑ₙ n^(n-1)/n!·Rⁿ` converges absolutely for `|R| < 1/e` (ratio `(1+1/n)^(n-1)·|R| → e·|R|`); the remaining M2 steps reconstruct the summable Mayer majorant from the Ursell tree bound and the Kotecký–Preiss `log Ξ = ∑` cluster identity. **Summable Mayer majorant** (`Penrose/SpanningTreeSummable.lean`, milestone M2): the real-analysis core `summable_completeGraph_numSpanningTrees_div_factorial_mul_pow` (`Summable (fun n => numSpanningTrees (⊤ : SimpleGraph (Fin n)) / n! · Rⁿ)` for `e·|R| < 1`, the explicit radius `1/e`) and its absolute form `_abs_pow`, obtained from the pure-power summability `summable_nat_pow_pred_div_factorial_mul_pow` (`Summable (fun n => n^(n-1)/n! · Rⁿ)`) by majorising `numSpanningTrees (⊤ Fin n) ≤ n^(n-1)`. The power series is summable by the **ratio test** (`summable_of_ratio_norm_eventually_le`): the eventual ratio is `(1+1/n)^(n-1)·|R| ≤ e·|R| < 1`, where the elementary bound `one_add_inv_natCast_pow_pred_le_exp` (`(1+1/n)^(n-1) ≤ e`, from `1+x ≤ eˣ` raised to the `(n-1)`-th power, no `(1+1/n)ⁿ → e` limit needed) feeds the post-cancellation inequality `succ_pow_pred_le_exp_mul_pow_pred` (`(n+1)^(n-1) ≤ e·n^(n-1)`). This supplies the convergent majorant for the Mayer/Ursell series; the remaining M2 steps are the Mayer-term reduction `Summable (mayerExpansionTerm G ·)` (Kotecký–Preiss) and `log Ξ = ∑` cluster. **Mayer expansion absolute convergence** (`MayerCore/UrsellMajorant.lean`, milestone M2 capstone): `summable_mayerExpansionTerm_of_exp_one_mul_activity_lt_one` (`Summable (fun n => mayerExpansionTerm G n t)` whenever `e·(∑_{P ∈ allPolymers G} |t|^{|P|}) < 1`) and the usable form `summable_mayerExpansionTerm_of_card_mul_lt` (sufficient: `|t| ≤ 1` and `e·|allPolymers G|·|t| < 1`). The chain: the Ursell coefficient is bounded uniformly `|ϕ^T(ω)| ≤ numSpanningTrees (⊤ Fin n)/n!` (`ursellCoefficient_abs_le_numSpanningTrees_top_div_factorial`, #3993); the total activity over `n`-tuples factorises `∑_{ω} |z(t,ω)| = (∑_{P} |t|^{|P|})^n` (`sum_clusterSeqActivity_abs_piFinset` via `Finset.sum_prod_piFinset`); so `|mayerExpansionTerm G n t| ≤ numSpanningTrees (⊤ Fin n)/n! · A^n` (`mayerExpansionTerm_abs_le_tree_activity_pow`) with `A = ∑_P |t|^{|P|}`, which is exactly the `SpanningTreeSummable` majorant at `R = A`. This is the absolute convergence `∑ₙ mayerExpansionTerm` of the cluster expansion for general interacting polymers; the remaining M2 steps are `log Ξ = ∑ₙ mayerExpansionTerm` (Kotecký–Preiss) and the high-temperature Ising specialisation `t = tanh(βJ)`. **High-temperature Ising convergence** (`MayerCore/MayerTanhConvergence.lean`, milestone M2): specialising to the Ising activity `t = tanh(β·J)`, `summable_mayerExpansionTerm_tanh` (`Summable (fun n => mayerExpansionTerm G n (tanh(β·J)))` for `e·|allPolymers G|·|tanh(β·J)| < 1`) with the ferromagnetic form `summable_mayerExpansionTerm_tanh_ferro` (`0 ≤ β·J`, `e·|allPolymers G|·tanh(β·J) < 1`) and the activity-sum form `_of_activity`; the ferromagnetic activity bound `tanh_activity_sum_le_card_mul_tanh` (`∑_P tanh(β·J)^{|P|} ≤ |allPolymers G|·tanh(β·J)`). Since `tanh` is continuous with `tanh 0 = 0`, the criterion holds at high temperature (`β·J` near `0`) — the Ising cluster expansion **converges absolutely at high temperature**. The remaining step is the Mayer–Montroll identity `log Ξ = ∑ₙ mayerExpansionTerm` (Issue #1499 Phase C). **Mayer–Montroll Phase C — colouring identity + `r!` fibre count** (`MayerCore/SurjectiveLogWeight.lean`, `MayerCore/MayerMontroll.lean`, Issue #1499, **Partial — tsum assembly remains**): the combinatorial core of the general-`t` Mayer identity, via a contraction-free edge inclusion–exclusion. **Surjective log-weight identity** (`SurjectiveLogWeight.lean`, graph-independent): `surjCount n k := #{surjections Fin n → Fin k}` with the surjection recurrence `surjCount_succ_succ` (`surjCount (n+1)(k+1) = (k+1)·(surjCount n (k+1) + surjCount n k)`, via two `Fin`/`Equiv` bijections); the telescoping identity `surjLogWeight_eq` (`∑_{k=1}^n (-1)^(k-1)/k · surjCount n k = [n = 1]`); and the general finite-type form `surjective_logWeight_eq_connected_indicator`. **Mayer–Montroll colouring identity** (`MayerMontroll.lean`, **done**): `mayerMontroll_coloring_identity` — `∑_{k=1}^r (-1)^(k-1)/k · #properSurjectiveColorings H k = alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum H` for any finite graph `H` on `Fin r`, via `badColorEdges`/`proper_indicator_eq_signed_bad_edges` (Boolean inclusion–exclusion over edges), `constantOnEdgeSet_iff_constant_on_components` + `colorings_constant_on_components_equiv` (the inner colour count factors through connected components), the edge expansion `properSurjectiveColorings_card_eq_sum_edges`, `surjLogWeight_sum_Icc_of_le`, and `connected_iff_card_connectedComponent_eq_one`. Bridged to the Ursell coefficient by `ursellCoefficient_eq_coloring_sum` (`ϕ^T(ω) = (∑_k (-1)^(k-1)/k · #properSurjectiveColorings (G(ω)) k)/n!`) and `mayerExpansionTerm_eq_coloring_form`. **`r!` colour-class fibre count** (`MayerMontroll.lean`, **done**): `card_proper_colorClass_fiber` — the `(ω, c)` (polymer sequence + proper colouring) with colour classes equal to a fixed family-tuple `Ω` number exactly `r!` (`r = ∑_a |Ω a|`), via the explicit `Equiv {(ω,c): proper, colorClass = Ω} ≃ (Fin r ≃ ↥labelledPolymers Ω)` (`seqColoringForward` bijection + `invColorClass`/`invProper`) and `Fintype.card_equiv`; the supporting `clusterSeqActivity_eq_prod_colorClass` (each fibre element has activity `∏_a ∏_{P∈Ω a} t^|P|`) and `fiber_sum_clusterSeqActivity` (`∑_{fibre} clusterSeqActivity = r!·W(Ω)`). **Per-`m` family-tuple identity** (`MayerMontroll.lean`, **done**): `vdFamilyTuple_sum_eq_seq_coloring_sum` regroups the family-tuple Mayer sum fibrewise over the colour-class map (`card_proper_colorClass_fiber` supplies the `r!` factor). **Capstone** (`MayerMontroll.lean`, **done**): `mayer_identity_general_t : polymerFreeEnergy G t = ∑'_n mayerExpansionTerm G n t` under the convergence hypotheses `|ε(t)| < 1` (log(1+ε) series) and `e·A < 1` (`A = ∑_P |t|^|P|`, double-sum Fubini), plus the unconditional eventual form `mayer_identity_general_t_eventually` (`∀ᶠ t in nhds 0`). The proof recasts both sides as the colour-degree double sum `colorDegreeTerm G t r k = (-1)^(k-1)/k · ∑_ω #properSurjectiveColorings(G(ω),k)/r! · clusterSeqActivity` (`mayerExpansionTerm_eq_tsum_colorDegreeTerm` row, `logTaylorTerm_eq_tsum_colorDegreeTerm` column), proves double-summability `summable_uncurry_colorDegreeTerm` (row majorant `∑_k|C(r,k)| ≤ (r^r/r!)·A^r` via `tsum_abs_colorDegreeTerm_le` + ratio test `summable_pow_self_div_factorial_mul_abs_pow`), and swaps the order with `Summable.tsum_comm` after shifting away the vanishing `k=0` column (`colorDegreeTerm_eq_zero_of_card_lt`). This completes GJ §18.4 (Mayer–Montroll / cluster identity `log Ξ = ∑ cluster terms`) in the convergence regime. |
| §18.5 analogy | **Interacting Ising free energy as a Mayer expansion** (Issue #3511) | **Implemented (interacting finite-volume free energy = convergent cluster expansion)** | `ClusterExpansion/MayerCore/InteractingFreeEnergyMayer.lean`. The interacting analogue of the non-interacting `freeEnergy_eq_tsum_mayer_of_pairwise_disjoint`: combining the polymer decomposition `freeEnergy_eq_polymerFreeEnergy` with the general Mayer–Montroll identity `mayer_identity_general_t`, `freeEnergy_eq_tsum_mayer_of_activity` gives, under the convergence hypotheses `|ε(tanh βJ)| < 1` and `e·∑_P |tanh βJ|^|P| < 1` (and `0 ≤ βJ`, `0 < |ι|`), `freeEnergy G ⟨J,0,β⟩ = log 2 + (|E|/|ι|)·log cosh(βJ) + (∑'_n mayerExpansionTerm G n (tanh βJ))/|ι|`. A ferromagnetic-hypothesis form `freeEnergy_eq_tsum_mayer_of_activity_ferromagnetic` (`0 ≤ J`, `0 < β`) is included. This removes the pairwise-vertex-disjoint restriction of the non-interacting case, expressing the interacting finite-volume free energy directly through its convergent cluster terms — the finite-volume step toward the §18.5 infinite-volume analyticity of the pressure (the convergence hypotheses' high-temperature discharge and the volume-uniform / infinite-volume limit are later work). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–335 (cluster-expansion convergence of the pressure / free energy). |
| §18.5 analogy | **High-temperature discharge of the interacting Mayer expansion** (Issue #3511) | **Implemented (interacting free energy = Mayer expansion under explicit high-temperature conditions)** | `ClusterExpansion/MayerCore/InteractingFreeEnergyMayerHighTemp.lean`. Discharges the two convergence hypotheses of `freeEnergy_eq_tsum_mayer_of_activity` from explicit finite-graph high-temperature smallness: `mayer_log_condition_of_one_add_pow_lt_two` (`|ε(t)|<1` from `(1+t)^|E|<2`, via `0≤ε≤(1+t)^|E|−1`) and `mayer_activity_condition_tanh_of_card_mul_lt` (`e·∑_P|tanh βJ|^|P|<1` from `e·|allPolymers G|·tanh(βJ)<1`, via `∑_P tanh^|P| ≤ |allPolymers G|·tanh`). The headline `freeEnergy_eq_tsum_mayer_of_high_temp` (+ `_ferromagnetic`) gives `freeEnergy G ⟨J,0,β⟩ = log 2 + (|E|/|ι|)·log cosh(βJ) + (∑'_n mayerExpansionTerm)/|ι|` under `(1+tanh βJ)^|E|<2 ∧ e·|allPolymers G|·tanh(βJ)<1` (both trivially satisfied as βJ→0), with no separate convergence hypotheses. Finite-volume; the volume-uniform / infinite-volume analyticity of the pressure is later work. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–335. |
| §18.5 analogy | **Λ-layer / along-exhaustion high-temperature Mayer expansion** (Issue #3511) | **Implemented (per-volume Λ and along-exhaustion wrappers)** | `AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/InteractingFreeEnergyMayerHighTemp.lean`. Λ-layer and along-exhaustion wrappers of `freeEnergy_eq_tsum_mayer_of_high_temp`: `freeEnergyΛ_eq_tsum_mayer_of_high_temp` (+ `_ferromagnetic`) and `freeEnergyAlongExhaustion_eq_tsum_mayer_of_high_temp` (+ `_ferromagnetic`) give, on a nonempty finite volume `Λ` (resp. each exhaustion stage `n`) satisfying the explicit high-temperature conditions on the induced graph, `freeEnergyΛ G Λ ⟨J,0,β⟩ = log 2 + (|E_Λ|/|Λ|)·log cosh(βJ) + (∑'_n mayerExpansionTerm (inducedGraph G Λ) n (tanh βJ))/|Λ|` (composing the core theorem at `inducedGraph G Λ` via `Finset.Nonempty.fintype_card_coe_pos`). These are **per-volume** statements: the high-temperature conditions `(1+tanh βJ)^|E_Λ|<2` and `e·|allPolymers(inducedGraph G Λ)|·tanh(βJ)<1` grow with `|Λ|`, so for fixed positive `tanh(βJ)` they fail at large volume — a volume-uniform Kotecky–Preiss / Dobrushin activity bound (not these per-volume conditions) is what a genuine infinite-volume Mayer pressure identity requires (later work). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–335. |
| §18.5 analogy | **Rooted polymer counting (volume-uniform max-degree bound)** (Issue #3511) | **Implemented (FV (3.49) rooted polymer count ≤ Δ^{2ℓ})** | `ClusterExpansion/PolymerCounting.lean`. The volume-uniform combinatorial input to the Kotecky–Preiss criterion: `rootedPolymers G v` (polymers whose support contains `v`), `rootedPolymersOfCard G v ℓ` (those of size `ℓ`), `rootedPolymersOfCard_card_le_closedWalkCount` (`(rootedPolymersOfCard G v ℓ).card ≤ (G.finsetWalkLength (2ℓ) v v).card`, via the FV §3.7.3 injection `card_connected_edge_sets_le` — polymers are connected edge sets, mapped to closed walks of length `2ℓ`), and the headline `rootedPolymersOfCard_card_le_maxDegree_pow` (`(rootedPolymersOfCard G v ℓ).card ≤ G.maxDegree^{2ℓ}`, chaining the walk injection with `walksFromCount_le_pow` via `degree_le_maxDegree`). This count depends only on the max degree, NOT on the volume — the first reusable volume-uniform object for the §18.5 infinite-volume cluster expansion (unlike the per-volume conditions of the high-temperature wrappers). The per-vertex activity geometric bound `∑_{P∋v} t^|P| ≤ (1−Δ²t)⁻¹` and the volume-uniform Mayer convergence are the next assembly. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–335; Friedli–Velenik §3.7.3 eq. (3.49). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Per-vertex polymer activity bound (volume-uniform)** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (geometric series `∑_{P∋v} t^|P| ≤ (1−Δ²t)⁻¹`)** | `ClusterExpansion/PolymerActivity.lean`. Sums the volume-uniform rooted polymer count of `PolymerCounting` against the activity weight. `rootedPolymerActivity G v t := ∑_{P ∈ rootedPolymers G v} t^|P|` and the headline `rootedPolymerActivity_le_geometric` (`0 ≤ t`, `Δ²t < 1 ⟹ rootedPolymerActivity G v t ≤ (1 − Δ²t)⁻¹`, `Δ = G.maxDegree`): partition `rootedPolymers G v` by cardinality (`Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to`), bound each size-`ℓ` fibre by `(rootedPolymersOfCard G v ℓ).card · t^ℓ ≤ Δ^{2ℓ}·t^ℓ = (Δ²t)^ℓ` via `rootedPolymersOfCard_card_le_maxDegree_pow`, then dominate the finite sum by the infinite geometric series `∑'_ℓ (Δ²t)^ℓ = (1−Δ²t)⁻¹` (`Summable.sum_le_tsum`, `tsum_geometric_of_lt_one`). The bound depends only on the maximum degree, not on the volume — the per-vertex Kotecky–Preiss activity input that survives the infinite-volume limit, unlike the per-volume conditions of `InteractingFreeEnergyMayerHighTemp`. Assembling the full volume-uniform Kotecky–Preiss convergence from it is later work. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–335; Friedli–Velenik §3.7.3 eq. (3.49). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Total / per-site polymer activity bound (volume-uniform)** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∑_P t^|P| ≤ |ι|·(1−Δ²t)⁻¹`; per-site `≤ (1−Δ²t)⁻¹`)** | `ClusterExpansion/PolymerActivity.lean`. Double-counts the per-vertex activity bound over all vertices. `allPolymersActivity G t := ∑_{P ∈ allPolymers G} t^|P|`; helper `one_le_card_polymerSupport_of_mem_allPolymers` (a polymer touches ≥ 1 vertex, via `Sym2.ind`/`Sym2.mem_mk_left`); `sum_rootedPolymerActivity_eq` (`∑_v ∑_{P∋v} t^|P| = ∑_P |supp P|·t^|P|`, by `Finset.sum_comm` + `Finset.sum_ite_mem`/`univ_inter`). Headline `allPolymersActivity_le_card_mul_geometric` (`0 ≤ t`, `Δ²t < 1 ⟹ ∑_P t^|P| ≤ |ι|·(1−Δ²t)⁻¹`): each polymer is counted at least once in the vertex sum (`1 ≤ |supp P|`), so the total activity is dominated by `∑_v rootedPolymerActivity G v t ≤ |ι|` copies of the per-vertex geometric bound. Corollary `allPolymersActivity_div_card_le_geometric` (`Nonempty ι`): the **per-site** activity `(1/|ι|)·∑_P t^|P| ≤ (1−Δ²t)⁻¹` — bounded by a volume-uniform constant depending only on `Δ = G.maxDegree`. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–335; Friedli–Velenik §3.7.3 eq. (3.49). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Weighted per-vertex + incompatibility-neighbourhood activity bounds (Kotecký–Preiss inputs)** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∑_{Q∼P}(e\|t\|)^\|Q\| ≤ \|supp P\|·(1−Δ²e\|t\|)⁻¹`)** | `ClusterExpansion/PolymerActivityKP.lean`. The local (per-vertex), `e`-weighted activity inputs to the Kotecký–Preiss criterion, built on `rootedPolymerActivity_le_geometric`. `rootedPolymerActivity_expWeighted_le_geometric` (`Δ²·e\|t\| < 1 ⟹ ∑_{Q∋v} e^\|Q\|·\|t\|^\|Q\| ≤ (1−Δ²e\|t\|)⁻¹`, the substitution `t ↦ e\|t\|`). `incompatiblePolymers G P := (allPolymers G).filter (PolymersIncompatible P)`; headline `incompatibilityActivity_expWeighted_le` (`∑_{Q∼P} (e\|t\|)^\|Q\| ≤ \|supp P\|·(1−Δ²e\|t\|)⁻¹`): each polymer incompatible with `P` shares a support vertex (`PolymersIncompatible.iff_exists_shared_vertex`), so the neighbourhood activity is bounded by `\|supp P\|` copies of the per-vertex geometric bound (witness-count `≥ 1` + `Finset.sum_comm` + subset of `rootedPolymers G v`). This `∑_{Q∼P}(e\|t\|)^\|Q\| ≤ \|supp P\|·(...)⁻¹` estimate is the direct input to the KP / rooted-cluster tree induction; both bounds are volume-uniform (max-degree only). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4, KP criterion), §5.7.1 (Ising application). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Kotecký–Preiss hypothesis discharged at high temperature (FV Thm 5.4)** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∑_{Q∼P} e^\|Q\|·\|t\|^\|Q\| ≤ \|P\|` for `Δ²e\|t\| ≤ ½`)** | `ClusterExpansion/PolymerActivityKP.lean`. Sharpens the activity bound to the *tail* geometric series and discharges the FV Theorem 5.4 KP hypothesis with weight `a(P) = \|P\|`. `rootedPolymerActivity_le_geometric_tail` (polymers are nonempty so the activity sum starts at edge-count 1: `∑_{Q∋v} u^\|Q\| ≤ (Δ²u)·(1−Δ²u)⁻¹`, the tail that vanishes as `u→0` — proved by the `ℓ=0` fibre being empty + `Summable.tsum_eq_zero_add`). `polymerSupport_card_le_card_of_mem_allPolymers` (`\|supp P\| ≤ \|P\|` via the handshake `sum_filter_card_eq_two_mul_card`: every support vertex of an even subgraph has even degree `≥ 2`, so `2\|supp P\| ≤ ∑_v deg = 2\|P\|`). Headline `incompatibilityActivity_expWeighted_le_card_of_half` (`Δ²e\|t\| ≤ ½ ⟹ ∑_{Q∼P} e^\|Q\|·\|t\|^\|Q\| ≤ \|P\|`): the tail per-vertex bound `Δ²e\|t\|·(1−Δ²e\|t\|)⁻¹ ≤ 1` (valid for `Δ²e\|t\| ≤ ½`) summed over the `≤ \|P\|` support vertices. This is the exact volume-uniform input that a Kotecký–Preiss cluster-expansion convergence theorem consumes. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Rooted-tree orientation of the polymer-sequence incompatibility graph (KP induction)** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (parent polymer incompatible with its child)** | `ClusterExpansion/Penrose/PolymerSeqTreeOrientation.lean`. The structural fact enabling the Kotecký–Preiss / tree-graph induction (FV Thm 5.4) to apply the discharged per-polymer activity hypothesis along the rooted-tree parent edges. Stated for any tree subgraph `G ≤ polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph ω`: `polymerSeqTree_parent_adj` (`(polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph ω).Adj v (treeParent hG r v hv)` — the rooted-tree parent adjacency in `G` transported along `G ≤ …`), `polymerSeqTree_parent_ne` (`parent ≠ v`), headline `polymerSeqTree_parent_incompatible` (`PolymersIncompatible (ω v) (ω (treeParent hG r v hv))` for non-root `v`, via `polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph_adj`). `fromEdgeSet_le_polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph` exhibits a spanning tree `T ∈ Penrose.spanningTreeEdgeSubsets (…)` as such a subgraph (`fromEdgeSet ↑T ≤ …`, from `T ⊆ edgeFinset`). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4, tree-graph inequality). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Parent-edge Kotecký–Preiss weight bound (rooted-tree induction input)** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`e^\|ω v\|·\|t\|^\|ω v\| ≤ \|ω (parent v)\|`)** | `ClusterExpansion/Penrose/PolymerSeqTreeKP.lean`. Combines the rooted-tree orientation (#4090) with the discharged KP hypothesis (#4089) into the per-edge input the tree-graph induction applies along every spanning-tree edge. For a tree subgraph `H ≤ polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph ω` of a sequence into `allPolymers G`: `polymerSeqTree_child_mem_incompatiblePolymers` (every non-root vertex's polymer `ω v ∈ incompatiblePolymers G (ω (treeParent hH r v hv))` — orientation recast in the `Finset` the KP activity sum ranges over, via `polymerSeqTree_parent_incompatible.symm`), and `polymerSeqTree_childActivityWeight_le_parentCard` (`Δ²e\|t\| ≤ ½ ⟹ e^\|ω v\|·\|t\|^\|ω v\| ≤ \|ω (parent v)\|`: the child's `e`-weight is a single term of `∑_{Q ∼ ω(parent)} e^\|Q\|\|t\|^\|Q\| ≤ \|ω(parent)\|` selected by `Finset.single_le_sum` + `incompatibilityActivity_expWeighted_le_card_of_half`). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Weighted spanning-tree sum dominated by a product of per-vertex sums** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∑_T ∏_i c i (parentCode T i) ≤ ∏_i ∑_p c i p`)** | `ClusterExpansion/Penrose/WeightedTreeSum.lean`. The combinatorial heart of the KP tree-graph bound — a *weighted* version of the parent-code count bound `numSpanningTrees (⊤ Fin (n+1)) ≤ (n+1)^n` (= `numSpanningTrees (⊤ Fin n) ≤ n^(n-1)`; the parent-function relaxation, weaker than Cayley's exact `(n+1)^(n-1)`), reusing the existing parent-code injection `completeGraphTreeParentCode_injective`. `completeGraphTreeParentCode_sum_le_sum_all_parentCodes` (`∑_{T spanning tree} F (parentCode T) ≤ ∑_{p : Fin n → Fin (n+1)} F p` for `0 ≤ F`, via `Finset.sum_image` + `Finset.sum_le_sum_of_subset_of_nonneg`: spanning trees inject into parent functions, so the tree sum is a sub-sum of the full parent-function sum). `completeGraphTreeParentCode_weighted_sum_le_prod_parentSums` (product weight `F p = ∏_i c i (p i)`: `∑_T ∏_i c i (parentCode T i) ≤ ∏_i ∑_p c i p`, factorising the unconstrained parent sum over independent vertex choices via `Fintype.prod_sum`). Converts a sum over the *constrained* spanning trees into a product of *independent* per-vertex sums — the latter being the per-vertex Kotecký–Preiss activity sums. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4, tree-graph inequality). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Monotonicity of spanning-tree edge subsets** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`spanningTreeEdgeSubsets G ⊆ spanningTreeEdgeSubsets H` for `G ≤ H`)** | `ClusterExpansion/Penrose/SpanningTreeMono.lean`. A spanning tree of a subgraph `G ≤ H` is a spanning tree of `H` (same vertices, edges in `G.edgeFinset ⊆ H.edgeFinset`, connectivity + edge-count unchanged): `spanningTreeEdgeSubsets_mono` (via `mem_spanningTreeEdgeSubsets` + `SimpleGraph.edgeFinset_subset_edgeFinset`), the weighted consequence `sum_spanningTree_le_of_le` (`0 ≤ F ⟹ ∑_{S ∈ spanningTreeEdgeSubsets G} F S ≤ ∑_{S ∈ spanningTreeEdgeSubsets H} F S`), and the `⊤` specialization `sum_spanningTree_le_top`. This lets a weighted sum over the spanning trees of any graph (e.g. a polymer incompatibility graph) be dominated by the same sum over the spanning trees of `⊤`, where the parent-code factorisation of `WeightedTreeSum` applies. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Mayer term bounded by the incompatibility-graph tree sum of activities** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`\|mayerExpansionTerm G n t\| ≤ (n!)⁻¹·∑_ω ∑_{T tree of incompat(ω)} ∏_i \|t\|^\|ω i\|`)** | `ClusterExpansion/MayerCore/MayerTreeSumActivity.lean`. `mayerExpansionTerm_abs_le_treeSum_activity`: bounds each Mayer term by a sum over the spanning trees of the polymer incompatibility graph of `ω` of the activity product, **keeping the incompatibility-graph tree range** (rather than collapsing to the volume-dependent `(∑_P \|t\|^\|P\|)^n` of `mayerExpansionTerm_abs_le_tree_activity_pow`), so the per-edge KP weight bound (#4091) can later be applied along the tree edges. Combines the triangle inequality `mayerExpansionTerm_abs_le`, the Penrose tree-graph Ursell bound `ursellCoefficient_abs_le_numSpanningTrees_div_factorial` (`\|ϕ^T(ω)\| ≤ numSpanningTrees(incompat ω)/n!`, on the incompatibility graph), and the activity factorisation `clusterSeqActivity_abs` (`\|z(t,ω)\| = ∏_i \|t\|^\|ω i\|`); the tree *count* is rewritten as a constant sum over `spanningTreeEdgeSubsets` to keep the trees explicit. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4, tree-graph inequality). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Inserting the KP `e`-weights into the Mayer tree-sum bound** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`\|mayerExpansionTerm G (n+1) t\| ≤ ((n+1)!)⁻¹·∑_ω ∑_T \|t\|^\|ω 0\|·∏_i e^\|ω(succ i)\|\|t\|^\|ω(succ i)\|`)** | `ClusterExpansion/MayerCore/MayerTreeSumExpActivity.lean`. `mayerExpansionTerm_succ_abs_le_treeSum_rootedExpActivity`: continues the rooted-tree assembly of #4094 by splitting off the root vertex `0` (`Fin.prod_univ_succ`) and inserting the KP weight `e^\|Q\| ≥ 1` (`one_le_pow₀` + `Real.one_le_exp_iff`) on the non-root factors of the activity product (valid since `\|t\|^\|Q\| ≥ 0`, `le_mul_of_one_le_left` + `Finset.prod_le_prod`). The non-root factors are now in exactly the per-edge KP weight form `e^\|Q\|·\|t\|^\|Q\|` of `polymerSeqTree_childActivityWeight_le_parentCard` (#4091), ready for the parent-edge reduction and the weighted spanning-tree factorisation (#4092/#4093). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4, tree-graph inequality). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Complete-tree parent edges are incompatibility edges (KP induction setup)** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`PolymersIncompatible (ω(succ i)) (ω(parentCode T i))`)** | `ClusterExpansion/Penrose/CompleteTreePolymerConstraint.lean`. The rooted-tree KP induction fixes a spanning-tree *shape* `T` of `K_{n+1}` (so `completeGraphTreeParentCode` is available) and sums over `ω` for which `T` is a spanning tree of `incompat(ω)`. `completeTree_parent_incompatible_of_mem_spanningTreeIncompat`: for such `ω`, every parent edge is an incompatibility edge (`PolymersIncompatible (ω(succ i)) (ω(parentCode T i))`) — the parent edge `s(succ i, parentCode T i) ∈ T` (`completeGraphTree_edges_eq_parentCode_image`) lies in `(incompat ω).edgeFinset`, so `polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph_adj` gives the incompatibility. `sum_filter_treeIncompat_le_filter_parentConstraint`: for `0 ≤ W`, the sum over `ω` with "`T` tree of `incompat ω`" is ≤ the sum over `ω` with the per-edge constraint "every child incompatible with its parent" (filter subset by the above). This relaxes the spanning-tree constraint to per-edge incompatibility — the starting point of the leaf-removal KP induction. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4, tree-graph inequality). |
| §18.5 analogy | **A complete-graph spanning tree has a non-root leaf** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∃ j, ∀ i, parentCode T i ≠ succ j`)** | `ClusterExpansion/Penrose/CompleteTreeLeaf.lean`. The rooted-tree KP induction peels leaves (vertices that are nobody's parent). `completeGraphTreeParentCode_exists_nonroot_leaf` (`n > 0`): the vertex `v` farthest from the root `0` (`Finset.exists_max_image` of `H.dist 0`) is a non-root leaf — `v ≠ 0` (else all vertices have distance `0`, so equal `0` by `Connected.dist_eq_zero_iff`, contradicting `n > 0`), and if `v = succ j` were the parent of some `succ i` then `treeParent_spec` gives `dist 0 (succ i) = dist 0 v + 1 > dist 0 v`, contradicting maximality. So `∀ i, completeGraphTreeParentCode n T i ≠ Fin.succ j`. This leaf can be summed out first in the induction. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4, tree-graph inequality). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Polynomial-moment bound for the geometric series** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∑_ℓ ℓ^d r^ℓ ≤ d!/(1−r)^{d+1}`)** | `ClusterExpansion/GeometricMoment.lean`. The analytic ingredient for the tree-route Kotecký–Preiss leaf-peel induction (summing a leaf into its parent leaves a `\|parent\|`-power factor; iterating accumulates polynomial moments of the per-vertex activity). `tsum_pow_mul_geometric_le` (`0 ≤ r < 1`): `∑'_ℓ ℓ^d r^ℓ ≤ d!·(1−r)^{−(d+1)}`. Each `ℓ^d` is dominated termwise by `d!·\binom{ℓ+d}{d}` (`ℓ^d ≤ (ℓ+1)^d ≤ (ℓ+d)^{\underline d} = d!·\binom{ℓ+d}{d}` via `Nat.pow_sub_le_descFactorial` + `Nat.descFactorial_eq_factorial_mul_choose`), and the negative-binomial geometric series sums to `(1−r)^{−(d+1)}` (`tsum_choose_mul_geometric_of_norm_lt_one`); termwise comparison by `Summable.tsum_le_tsum` with `summable_pow_mul_geometric_of_norm_lt_one`. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Per-vertex polymer activity moment bound (volume-uniform)** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∑_{Q∋v} \|Q\|^d u^\|Q\| ≤ d!/(1−Δ²u)^{d+1}`)** | `ClusterExpansion/PolymerActivityMoment.lean`. The per-vertex `d`-th activity moment, volume-uniform (the leaf-peel induction accumulates these as each peeled leaf leaves a `\|parent\|`-power factor). `rootedPolymerActivity_cardPow_le` (`0 ≤ u`, `Δ²u < 1`): `∑_{Q∋v} \|Q\|^d u^\|Q\| ≤ d!·(1−Δ²u)^{−(d+1)}` — partition the rooted polymers by cardinality (`Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to`, mirroring #4086), bound each size-`ℓ` fibre by `Δ^{2ℓ}·ℓ^d·u^ℓ = ℓ^d·(Δ²u)^ℓ` via the volume-uniform count `rootedPolymersOfCard_card_le_maxDegree_pow`, then dominate the finite sum by the geometric-moment series `∑'_ℓ ℓ^d (Δ²u)^ℓ ≤ d!/(1−Δ²u)^{d+1}` (#4098 `tsum_pow_mul_geometric_le`, via `Summable.sum_le_tsum`). The `d=0` case is `rootedPolymerActivity_le_geometric`. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4). |
| §18.5 analogy | **First-coordinate separation of a constant `piFinset` sum** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∑_{ω∈piFinset} f ω = ∑_x ∑_{ω'} f (cons x ω')`)** | `ClusterExpansion/PiFinsetSeparation.lean`. The coordinate-peeling step for the rooted-tree KP leaf-peel induction, which sums over polymer sequences `ω : Fin (n+1) → X` in a constant `Fintype.piFinset` one coordinate at a time. `sum_piFinset_const_succ`: `∑_{ω ∈ piFinset (fun _:Fin (n+1) => s)} f ω = ∑_{x ∈ s} ∑_{ω' ∈ piFinset (fun _:Fin n => s)} f (Fin.cons x ω')`. The `piFinset` is the image of `s ×ˢ piFinset` under the injection `Fin.cons` (`Fin.consEquiv`); the proof establishes the set equality by `ext` (forward: `x = ω 0`, `ω' = Fin.tail ω`, glued by `Fin.cons_self_tail`; backward: `Fin.cases` on the index) then `Finset.sum_map` + `Finset.sum_product`. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.5, pp. 332–336; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4). |
| §18.5 analogy | **Arbitrary-coordinate separation of a constant `piFinset` sum** (Issue #3954) | **Implemented (`∑_ω f ω = ∑_x ∑_{ω'} f (p.insertNth x ω')`)** | `ClusterExpansion/PiFinsetSeparationPivot.lean`. Generalises #4100 to an arbitrary pivot `p : Fin (n+1)` (the leaf-peel peels an arbitrary leaf, not just index 0). `sum_piFinset_const_insertNth (p)`: `∑_{ω ∈ piFinset (fun _:Fin (n+1) => s)} f ω = ∑_{x ∈ s} ∑_{ω' ∈ piFinset (fun _:Fin n => s)} f (p.insertNth x ω')`.
