Uses `pow_le_pow_left₀` for the monotonicity in the base. Step C of the cubic CE-route plumbing. **`correlationComplex` Lipschitz on `closedBall` from `Z ≠ 0` + deriv bound** (Step D-light): `correlationComplex_lipschitz_on_closedBall` (`ComplexAnalyticity/Correlation.lean`) gives the explicit Lipschitz bound `‖corrC w − corrC b‖ ≤ C · ‖w − b‖` for `w, b ∈ closedBall β₀ R`, given `Z_ℂ ≠ 0` on the closed disc (analyticity) and an explicit deriv bound `C` (from the Cauchy estimate at a larger outer radius). Pure composition of `correlationComplex_analyticAt_beta` + `convex_closedBall` + mean-value. The deriv bound `C` itself is supplied via `correlationComplex_norm_deriv_le_of_norm_le_on_sphere` (#3052) + `correlationComplex_norm_le_of_partition_norm_ge`. Step D of the cubic CE-route plumbing (light version: deriv bound delegated to user). **Cubic abs uniform geometric high-temperature** (Step A + Step C composition, PR #3134): `abs_correlation_inducedGraph_cubic_succ_sub_le_uniform_geometric_high_temp` combines the cf_max uniformization (#3122) with the floor → geometric conversion (#3119) to give the fully-simplified bound `|c_k − c_{k+1}| ≤ (1/cf_max) · (2k+3)^d · ρ_R_max^{k+1−R}` with explicit `ρ_R_max := cf_max^{1/(r₀+2)} ∈ (0, 1)`. β_re-independent and fully geometric. **CE-route scope clarification + direct increment API** (refactor Direction 1+3 per codex strategic review, PRs #3125-#3126): The CE-route bundles are now documented as **structural bridges from per-(β, k) complex circle bound to summable increment**, not capstones in the per-fixed-volume regime — the smallness condition cannot be satisfied by the currently-available Cauchy-derived data when the disc radius shrinks with volume. For users with a direct increment bound (from any non-CE route), `CERouteIccDirectGeometricIncrement` / `CERouteIccDirectPolyGeometricIncrement` (named aliases for the `hincr` shape) and the corresponding `lemma_17_5_2_{upper_bound,capstone}_of_CERouteIccDirect{,Poly}GeometricIncrement` pass-throughs to `IncrementCapstone.lean` provide the structurally explicit entry point. Open: R_inc and C themselves (Issue #3044 Simon-Lieb / Cauchy estimate); volume-uniform direction (single `r` across stages) still requires sharper Q-bounds. The CE-route structural Props (cluster-expansion convergence in complex) and the volume-uniform circle bound `B` (complex Simon-Lieb) remain the open inputs for the volume-uniform direction. Foundational steps for extending the cluster expansion to a complex disc in `β`/`J`, en route to the volume-uniform `Z_ℂ` lower bound for the Lemma 17.5.2 `hZ` provider (#3044). (Steps 555-565, 575; PRs #1528-#1541, #1608-#1613; Issue #1344, Issue #3054.) |
| §18.6 analogy | **High-temperature interval analyticity / no phase transition** (Issue #4149) | **Conditional / limited-range: open-interval analyticity + smooth thermodynamic functions** | `ClusterExpansion/MayerCore/CubicMayerHighTempIntervalAnalyticity.lean`. Upgrades the single-point project §18.6 lattice-analogue endpoint `freeEnergyInfinite_latticeGraph_cubicExhaustion_analyticAt_beta_h_zero` (#4160) to analyticity on the **whole high-temperature interval**: for `J > 0` and Kotecký–Preiss parameters `T, R`, `freeEnergyInfinite_latticeGraph_cubicExhaustion_analyticOnNhd_high_temp` proves `AnalyticOnNhd ℝ (fun β' => freeEnergyInfinite (latticeGraph d) (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J,0,β'⟩) (Set.Ioo 0 (artanh T / J))` — because `β < artanh T / J` is exactly `tanh(β J) < T` (via `Real.tanh_strictMono` + `Real.tanh_artanh`). `..._analyticOnNhd_unitCoupling` is the `J=1` form on `Ioo 0 (artanh T)`. The physical no-phase-transition consequences: `..._continuousOn_high_temp` (continuous), `..._contDiffOn_high_temp` (`C^∞`, indeed `C^ω`, no singularity in the phase), `..._energyDensity_analyticOnNhd_high_temp` (the internal energy density `∂f/∂β` is real-analytic), `..._specificHeat_analyticOnNhd_high_temp` (the specific heat `∂²f/∂β²` is real-analytic). So the infinite-volume thermodynamic functions are real-analytic throughout the high-temperature phase, i.e. **no phase transition at high temperature**. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.6. |
| §18.6 analogy | **Coupling-direction (`J`) high-temperature analyticity** (Issue #4149) | **Conditional / limited-range: lattice KP analogue, not literal GJ Chapter 18** | `ClusterExpansion/MayerCore/CubicMayerCouplingDirectionAnalyticity.lean` derives pointwise and interval analyticity in `J` from the zero-field scaling symmetry `freeEnergyInfinite ⟨J,0,β⟩ = freeEnergyInfinite ⟨β·J,0,1⟩`, together with continuity, `C^∞` smoothness, and analyticity of the bond-energy density. Primary source: Friedli–Velenik §5.4 and §5.7; GJ Chapter 18 is only the analogy label defined above. |
| §18.5 / §18.6 analogy | **High-temperature discharge of the Kotecký–Preiss conditions** (Issue #4214) | **Conditional / limited-range: single threshold `Δ²e\|t\| < 1/6` ⟹ both KP conditions; §18.5 convergence + §18.6 analyticity made unconditional** | `ClusterExpansion/HighTempKoteckyPreiss.lean`. The §18.5 Mayer-series convergence and §18.6 free-energy analyticity were stated under the two Kotecký–Preiss tail conditions `hkp : Δ²e\|t\| < 1` and `hρ : 4Δ²e\|t\|/(1−Δ²e\|t\|)² < 1` (`Δ = G.maxDegree`, `t` the activity, `e = exp 1`). `kp_tail_conditions_of_lt` discharges **both from the single clean threshold** `r = Δ²e\|t\| < 1/6`: `r < 1/6 < 1` gives `hkp`, and `4r/(1−r)² < 1 ⟺ 4r < (1−r)² ⟺ 0 < (1−6r)+r²` holds since `1−6r > 0` and `r² ≥ 0` (`nlinarith`); `1/6 ≈ 0.1667` is a clean sufficient bound just below the tight `3−2√2 ≈ 0.1716`, avoiding `√2`. Graph-level `kp_tail_conditions_of_activity_lt`. Unconditional §18.5 convergence: `summable_mayerExpansionTerm_of_activity_lt`, `tendsto_mayerPartialSum_of_activity_lt`. Per-site `tsum_abs_mayerExpansionTerm_succ_div_card_le_of_activity_lt` and ℤ^d cubic `latticeGraph_kp_tsum_per_site{,_cubicExhaustion}_of_activity_lt` (from `(2d)²e\|t\| < 1/6`). Unconditional project §18.6 lattice-analogue analyticity: `freeEnergyInfinite_latticeGraph_cubicExhaustion_analyticAt_beta_h_zero_of_activity` (pointwise) and `…_analyticOnNhd_high_temp_of_activity`, `…_analyticOnNhd_unitCoupling_of_activity` (whole high-temperature interval) from the two thresholds `(2d)²eR < 1/6`, `(2d)²eT < 1/6`. The derived-regularity corollaries and coupling-direction analyticity are discharged in `ClusterExpansion/HighTempKoteckyPreissRegularity.lean` (9 wrappers): β-direction `…_continuousOn_high_temp_of_activity`, `…_contDiffOn_high_temp_of_activity`, `…_energyDensity_high_temp_of_activity` (internal energy density `∂_β f`), `…_specificHeat_high_temp_of_activity` (`∂_β² f` — no singularity); and the coupling-direction (`J`) family `…_analyticAt_J_h_zero_of_activity`, `…_analyticOnNhd_J_high_temp_of_activity`, `…_continuousOn_J_high_temp_of_activity`, `…_contDiffOn_J_high_temp_of_activity`, `…_couplingEnergy_J_high_temp_of_activity` (bond energy density `∂_J f`). After these, **every public project §18.6 lattice-analogue high-temperature analyticity/regularity statement is free of the explicit `hkp`/`hρ` hypotheses**, depending only on the threshold `(2d)²e·{R,T} < 1/6`. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.4–§18.6, pp. 332–340; Friedli–Velenik §5.4 (Theorem 5.4, Kotecký–Preiss). |
| §18.6 analogy | **Existence of a high-temperature analyticity threshold (no phase transition)** (Issue #4214) | **Conditional / limited-range: `∃ β₀ > 0` explicit; free energy + specific heat + coupling analytic on `(0, β₀)`** | `ClusterExpansion/HighTempAnalyticityCapstone.lean`. Exhibits an explicit Kotecký–Preiss radius `kpRadius d = 1/(7·((2d)²+1)·e)` (`+1` keeps the denominator positive for all `d`) and packages the project §18.6 lattice-analogue analyticity as clean **existential** statements free of the auxiliary `R, T, hkp, hρ` machinery. `kpRadius_pos`, `kpRadius_lt_one`, `kpRadius_threshold` (`(2d)²e·kpRadius d = (2d)²/(7((2d)²+1)) < 1/7 < 1/6`, via `field_simp` + `div_lt_iff₀` + `nlinarith`). `exists_high_temp_freeEnergyInfinite_analyticOnNhd` — for every `d` and `J > 0`, `∃ β₀ > 0` (`= artanh(kpRadius d)/J`, positive by `Real.artanh_pos`) with the infinite-volume ℤ^d Ising free energy real-analytic in `β` on `(0, β₀)`. `exists_high_temp_specificHeat_analyticOnNhd` — the **specific heat `∂_β² f` is real-analytic on `(0, β₀)`: no singularity, hence no phase transition** at high temperature. `exists_high_temp_freeEnergyInfinite_analyticOnNhd_J` — the coupling-direction analog `∃ J₀ > 0` for fixed `β > 0`. The energy-density existentials `exists_high_temp_energyDensity_analyticOnNhd` (`∂_β f`) and `exists_high_temp_couplingEnergy_analyticOnNhd_J` (`∂_J f`) cover the remaining first-derivative observables. **Combined headlines** `exists_high_temp_no_phase_transition` / `…_J` — a *single* explicit threshold `β₀` (resp. `J₀`) makes the free energy **and** its first/second derivatives (internal energy density `∂_β f`, specific heat `∂_β² f` / bond energy density `∂_J f`) *all simultaneously* real-analytic on `(0, β₀)` (resp. `(0, J₀)`): the complete "no thermodynamic singularity" statement of the high-temperature phase. The cleanest form of the project §18.6 lattice-analogue high-temperature analyticity headline. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §18.6, pp. 335–340; Friedli–Velenik §5.4. |
| §18.7 analogy | Decay of correlations | **Conditional / limited-range: high-temperature lattice results at `h = 0`, with connected two-point extensions for `h ≥ 0`** | `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_le_numerator` (Step 566): under `0 ≤ β·J`, `⟨σ_A⟩ ≤ ∑_{X : ∂X = A} tanh(β·J)^{\|X\|}` reduces capstone to numerator. `evenSubgraph_pair_boundary_card_pos` (Step 567): `1 ≤ X.card`. `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_tanh` (Step 568): weak upper bound `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩ ≤ 2^{\|E\|} · tanh(β·J)`. Foundation lemmas: `evenSubgraph_pair_boundary_exists_edge_incident_to` (Step 569), `filter_mem_card_erase` (Step 570), `evenSubgraph_pair_boundary_card_one_adj` (Step 571), `evenSubgraph_pair_boundary_erase_swap` (Step 572). `evenSubgraph_pair_boundary_dist_le` (Step 573): graph-distance bound `G.dist i j ≤ X.card` under `∂X = {i, j}` via strong induction building an explicit `G.Walk i j`. **`correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_tanh_pow_dist` (Step 574, capstone)**: `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩ ≤ 2^{\|E\|} · tanh(β·J)^{d_G(i,j)}` under `0 ≤ β·J` — high-temperature exponential decay in graph distance. Combines Steps 566+573 with `tanh ≤ 1` reduction (`pow_le_pow_of_le_one`). **Λ-layer + along-ex + ℤ^d wraps** (PR #1570): `correlationΛ_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_tanh_pow_dist` (`AmbientLattice/Defs.lean`) + `correlationAlongExhaustion_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_tanh_pow_dist` (`AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/HighTemperatureBoundsDecayCapstones.lean`, narrowed out of `HighTemperatureBounds.lean` in PR #2000, per-stage `n`) + `correlationΛ_latticeGraph_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_tanh_pow_dist` and `correlationAlongExhaustion_latticeGraph_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_tanh_pow_dist` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/HighTemperatureBoundsDecayCapstones.lean`, on `latticeGraph d`). **Ferromagnetic forms** (PR #1571, under `0 ≤ J, 0 < β`): abstract `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_tanh_pow_dist_ferromagnetic` plus the four-layer wraps `correlationΛ_..._ferromagnetic`, `correlationAlongExhaustion_..._ferromagnetic`, `correlationΛ_latticeGraph_..._ferromagnetic`, `correlationAlongExhaustion_latticeGraph_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_tanh_pow_dist_ferro` (the last variant drops `_high_temp` and uses `_ferro` instead of `_ferromagnetic` to keep the name under the `linter.style.longLine` 100-character budget). **Rate-form package** (PR #1842): `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_exp_rate_dist` rewrites the capstone as `2^{\|E\|} * exp(-(-log(tanh(β·J))) * d_G(i,j))`, with Λ, along-exhaustion, ℤ^d, and ferromagnetic wrappers. The endpoint `tanh(β·J)=0` is handled as a valid inequality using Lean's total `Real.log 0 = 0`. **Monotone-rate package** (PR #1843): `correlation_high_temp_h_zero_at_pair_le_two_pow_edges_mul_exp_alpha_dist` lets any real `α ≤ -log(tanh(β·J))` replace the exact rate, with matching Λ, along-exhaustion, ℤ^d, and ferromagnetic wrappers. The proof is monotonicity of `Real.exp` after multiplying `-(-log(tanh(β·J))) ≤ -α` by the nonnegative graph distance. **Named-rate API** (PR #1844): `highTempExpRate β J := -log(tanh(β·J))`, with zero-slice simp lemmas, nonnegativity under `0 ≤ β·J`, a ferromagnetic bridge, and named-rate forms of the exact-rate and monotone-rate bounds. **Named-rate layer wrappers** (PR #1845): Λ, along-exhaustion, and ℤ^d wrappers for both the exact named-rate bound and the named monotone-rate bound `α ≤ highTempExpRate β J`. **Named-rate ferromagnetic lightweight wrappers** (PR #1846): ferromagnetic convenience wrappers for the named exact-rate and named monotone-rate APIs at the abstract, Λ, along-exhaustion, and ℤ^d layers, with the along-exhaustion and concrete additions split into lightweight `AmbientLattice/CorrelationDecay.lean` and `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CorrelationDecay.lean` modules to avoid importing the analytic cluster-expansion stack during incremental checks. (PRs #1570, #1571, #1842, #1843, #1844, #1845, #1846; Issue #1344.) **General external-field connected-correlation subset form** (`Conditioning/CorrelationClosed/TruncatedGeneralField.lean`): the connected (truncated) correlation functions — the objects whose decay the cluster expansion controls — at a general external field `h`, expressed through the §18.3 master subset expansion. Named building blocks `highTempSubsetNumerator G p F` (`= ∑_{X⊆E} tanh(βJ)^{\|X\|} ∑_σ F(σ)(∏_{e∈X}σ_e) e^{βh∑σ}`) and `highTempSubsetDenominator G p` (the `F = 1` case `= Z/cosh(βJ)^{\|E\|}`), with `gibbsExpectation_eq_highTempSubsetNumerator_div_denominator` / `correlation_eq_highTempSubsetNumerator_div_denominator`. `truncated2_high_temp_expansion_general_h_subset_form`: `⟨σ_i;σ_j⟩_p = N[σ_{ij}]/D − (N[σ_i]/D)(N[σ_j]/D)`; `truncated3_high_temp_expansion_general_h_subset_form`: the three-point Ursell function in the same form. Finite-volume lattice Ising identities (no continuum limit), the general-`h` foundation for connected-correlation decay. (Analogous to GJ §18; primary source Friedli–Velenik §3.7.3/§5.3–5.7.) **General external-field exponential decay of the connected 2-point function** (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Truncated2GeneralFieldDecay.lean`): on `ℤ^d` for ferromagnetic `⟨J,h,β⟩` with `h ≥ 0`, the connected `⟨σ_i;σ_j⟩ = truncated2Infinite` (which, unlike the full 2-point, decays at `h ≠ 0`) inherits the `h = 0` Simon–Lieb decay via GHS field-monotonicity. `truncated2Infinite_latticeGraph_le_betaJ_two_d_pow_of_ferromagnetic_field_nonneg` (`≤ (βJ·2d)^n` for `n+1 ≤ d_{ℤ^d}(i,j)`), `_le_exp_neg_simonLiebRate_pow_of_ferromagnetic_field_nonneg` (`≤ exp(−(−log(βJ·2d))·n)`, high-temp), and `_le_exp_neg_half_simonLiebRate_of_ferromagnetic_field_nonneg` (half-rate, full distance, `d_{ℤ^d}(i,j) ≥ 2`). Proof: `truncated2Infinite_antitoneOn_h_of_ne` (GHS, `h≥0` ⟹ `truncated2Infinite(h) ≤ truncated2Infinite(0)`) + `truncated2Infinite_h_zero` (`= correlationInfinite(0){i,j}` since `⟨σ_i⟩=0`) + existing `h=0` Simon–Lieb decay. Lattice Ising, no continuum limit. (GJ §18.7, pp. 340–343; §4.3 Cor. 4.3.4 GHS.) **General external-field cluster property** (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Truncated2GeneralFieldCluster.lean`): on `ℤ^d` for `0 < J, 0 ≤ h, 0 < β, βJ·2d < 1`, the connected `j ↦ truncated2Infinite ⟨J,h,β⟩ i j → 0` along cofinite (i.e. as `d_{ℤ^d}(i,j) → ∞`). `HasExponentialDecay_of_field_nonneg_high_temp` lifts the `h=0` exponential-decay witness `hasExponentialDecay_latticeGraph_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` to general `h` via GHS field-monotonicity (same `(C, −log(βJ·2d))` witness, since `0 ≤ truncated2Infinite(h) ≤ truncated2Infinite(0)` for `h ≥ 0` distinct sites), and `clusterProperty_latticeGraph_of_field_nonneg_high_temp` feeds it to `clusterProperty_latticeGraph_of_HasExponentialDecay`. Lattice Ising, no continuum limit. (GJ §18.7, pp. 340–343; §4.3 Cor. 4.3.4 GHS.) |
